# The Agent Frontier — what is still missing, and the mechanism for each

*2026-07-31. The third document. `AGENT_PERFECTION_2026-07-30.md` fixed what was
broken (W1–W10, M1–M5). `AGENT_HORIZON_2026-07-31.md` mapped what must exist
(P1–P8, I1–I5, U1–U6, A1–A4, X1–X5, S1–S5). This one is the sweep those two did
not reach: seven researchers, seven corners, every claim grounded in a file:line
or a URL. Where an item deepens an existing one it says so by id and adds only
the evidence or mechanism the original lacks. Nothing here restates them.*

---

## The thesis — what "a really cool agent" means for THIS product

Not "it can do more things". Six properties, every one of them named by the
findings below rather than by taste:

**1. It sees what you see.** Right now it cannot. Checklist items are outside
scope (DA4), so three shipped verbs can never resolve an existing task.
Connectors are outside scope (DA5), so the flow-layout engine's edge reader is
literally dead code. Comment threads are outside scope (DA6), so the board's
actual decisions are invisible. It cannot see a card's size (CV19), a heading it
made (CV18), the filter the person is looking through (CV22), the direction they
pinned (CV6), or whether a formula evaluates to 4,900 or to the string
`=SUM(B2:B9)` (CV10). The digest's own comment states the law — *a write
capability without the matching read is how a parallel taxonomy gets born* — and
the tree breaks it in nine places.

**2. Every capability it has actually lands on the board.** `set_color` writes a
key no renderer reads (CV1). `place_attachment` turns a PDF into a broken image
(CV2). `set_reminder` on anything but a TASK is accepted, committed, and never
fires (DA9). `create_link` explicitly sets `showPreview:false`, so an agent-made
link is *worse* than a default one (DA10). Four shipped, prompted, evaluated
capabilities that pass every test and change nothing a person sees — because no
probe in the corpus asserts a **rendered** result.

**3. The preview shows the thing being previewed.** Connect actions are excluded
from the layout pass and never render as ghosts (CV3) — so the diagram capability
previews as loose boxes with the arrows, which *are* the diagram, missing. Cards
inside columns never dim (CV4), so a plan that empties a column previews as a
column that is fine. M1 closed this one level down; it is still open one level
further in.

**4. Corrections teach.** The product captures the best correction signal of any
tool surveyed — typed per-row Adjustments (drop/retitle/retext/reparent), Refine,
per-run and per-action Revert — and discards all of it at commit (LP1, CG7).
Discarded plans, the cheapest labelled data in the industry, are written to a
field nobody queries (LP4). Meanwhile the one preference store that exists is a
string the frontend concatenates and the digest truncates at 600 characters, so
the seventh rule silently evicts the first (CG5), and the account-wide rules box
the settings UI advertises reaches exactly one destination in the whole
codebase — an anti-injection whitelist (CG1).

**5. It behaves correctly when the board has other people on it.** Boards are
shared; the agent is single-tenant throughout. Run events — including raw intent,
refinement notes and steer text — are broadcast to every viewer in the room,
including anonymous share-link holders (MP3), while the HTTP path one file away
carefully redacts the same data. Ancestry enrichment walks up past boards the
principal cannot read (MP4). The one-run-per-board lock is keyed on the root id
while the blast radius is the whole subtree (MP5). Cross-run memory is
tenant-scoped, so W6's fix and G2's 5-in-6 reliability are both single-player
only (MP7). And the entire eval corpus seeds one owner, one board (MP11) — the
exact argument M5 made about flatland, one axis over.

**6. It is measurable.** Two probes are known "probabilistic at the margin" and
that rate was established by hand, because the harness runs each probe once
(CG4). Every item in the cognition corner changes a *distribution*, and a
single-sample harness cannot see one.

And the strategic read from the industry corner: in 2026 every serious canvas
product converged on three moves. Plan-then-approve agents on the canvas — which
QomraNote shipped earlier and better. User-authored agent skills — which
QomraNote has one anonymous instance of (LP15). And opening the board to
**external** agents over MCP with permissions (Miro + Anthropic, Figma
`get_figjam`/`generate_diagram`, Notion External Agents). QomraNote has none of
the third and the best possible substrate for it: staged writes, ghost preview,
one-transaction commit, per-run revert, and delegation attenuation. It is the
only product in this survey that can grant an external agent an authority the
others cannot even express — **propose, but not apply** (PL17).

---

## How to read this

Seven corners, tiered inside each. **T1** = parity or a live wrong; the agent
looks broken without it. **T2** = collaborator, not tool. **T3** = the frontier.
🔴 marks a **confirmed live wrong** — code that misbehaves today, not a gap.
Three findings in the DA corner were proved empirically with throwaway Go probes
against the in-memory repositories (probe files run, then deleted; no tree
changes remain) and are marked **PROVEN**.

The five-clause contract from HORIZON is the law: **schema** (what the model may
say), **handler** (authorization + containment), **compiler** (the keys the
renderer reads), **digest** (the read that makes revision possible), **probe**
(an eval that exercises it in a nested workspace). Every T1 and T2 item names
its **hard clause** — the one that carries the risk. A striking pattern in what
follows: the hard clause is *digest* far more often than *schema*. This product's
agent is not short of verbs. It is short of sight.

---

## Corrections to the two prior documents

Read these before planning any wave; four of them would send a wave in the wrong
direction.

| Prior item | Claim | Correction | Item |
|---|---|---|---|
| **X1** | Import "rides the existing AttachmentIDs path" | **False.** `vision.go:41-50` allows png/jpeg/webp/gif/pdf only; `planner.go:410-419` routes *all* request attachments through that fetcher and journals `EvError` for the rest. Attach a markdown brief and the run silently loses it. | PL3 |
| **X2** | Export "needs an export endpoint" | **Exists** — `router.go:27`, `board_service.go:197`, markdown/text/json. The real defect is that the renderer linearises by y-then-x geometry and silently drops TABLE, SKETCH, ALIAS, CLONE, COMMENT_THREAD, LINE. | PL11 |
| **S4** | "per-day account caps do not exist" | **They do** — `config.go:55`, `service.go:294`. The gaps are: one dimension, admission-time only, a 500-doc scan, and unenforceable on an unpriced model. | PL6 |
| **P2** | The agent "omits embed metadata" | Worse — `plan.go:781-784` explicitly writes `showPreview:false`. And `LinkService.Resolve` is *already* held by the planner (`planner.go:361`), SSRF-hardened, returning every key `content` needs. | DA10 |
| **P4** | The digest "must render BOTH formula and value" | Today it renders **only the raw formula** (`digest.go:1152`), so "is the budget over?" reads the string `=SUM(B2:B9)` and has no number. The engine is frontend-only; a Go port with shared fixtures is the prerequisite. | CV10 |
| **P3** | Templates: add `use_template` | Two missing halves: `isTemplate` is invisible to the scope walk, so "organise everything" will happily reorganise your blank sprint template; and `UseTemplate` writes *outside* the transaction path — no ops, no undo, no ghosts. | DA12 |
| **P7** | Icon vocabulary "served the way cardSwatches is" | `cardSwatches` is itself broken (CV1), so it is the wrong template to copy. Board tiles also take **gradients**, not card pastels — serving a flat pastel produces a tile that looks broken beside its siblings. | CV20 |
| **P8** | `list_trash` "(read, owner-scoped)" | The panel's model is per-**actor**, not per-owner, and restore is **batch**-scoped: one restore call can resurrect a colleague's deliberate cleanup and far more than the item named. | DA18 |
| **U2** | Mark the selection with ⭐ | The real defect is the inverse: in `ScopeSelection` everything **not** selected vanishes from scope (`digest.go:375`), so "file these under the budget column" cannot resolve a column the person can see on screen. | CV8 |
| **U4** | Presence is "pure FE composition over events already emitted" | Not buildable as written: presence is bound to a live `*Client` (`hub.go:84`) and a run has no socket; and the event stream it would read is the one MP3 must redact. Needs a server-side virtual member. | MP16 |
| **U5** | The agent should answer in the request's language | The deeper fact: the chrome **never mirrors**. `setLanguage` sets `<html lang>` and nothing sets `dir`. And the durable fix for style is a derived measurement, not a prompt sentence. | CV9, CG10 |
| **U6** | Surface ambient suggestions in the empty composer | The trigger design is right; it never learns. A hint dismissed five times keeps appearing, and the `Kind` field's own comment asks for telemetry nothing writes. | LP11 |
| **W6 / G2** | Memory between runs; "complete" as a follow-up | Both are **tenant-scoped** (`service.go:1106`). The block is empty when the previous run was a colleague's — so the duplicate-structure failure W6 documents is still live on any shared board. | MP7 |
| **M5** | The corpus never leaves flatland | Same argument, two more axes: it never leaves **single-player** (MP11) and it never runs a probe **twice** (CG4). | MP11, CG4 |
| **I1** | Embeddings as the semantic-sight substrate | Right, and it has no named consumer. Two exist: relevance-ordered scope admission (CG12) and clustering as a first-class verb (IN1) — the cheapest thing that makes the migration pay. | CG12, IN1 |
| **S1/S2** | Provider fallback; run queue | Both restated below with mechanisms: fallback is a degenerate case of per-phase model routing (CG8); the queue's real blocker is that the lock is keyed wrong (MP5). | CG8, MP5 |

---

# Corner CV — Capability landing, canvas, and the review surface

*Method: every file under `frontend/src` (11,430 lines, 63 files), cross-checked
against the tool catalogue, digest, op compiler and handlers. The highest-value
cluster is CV1–CV5: shipped capabilities that write keys nothing renders, target
ids that can never resolve, or produce previews that omit the thing being
previewed. No eval catches any of them, because no probe asserts a rendered
result.*

## T1 — parity and live wrongs

#### CV1 · `set_color` writes a key no renderer reads — the whole swatch capability is a silent no-op 🔴
- **Evidence** — `plan.go:671` compiles `content:{"color":hex}`; `NoteCard.tsx:109` reads `element.content?.backgroundColor`; the swatch table `tools.go:114-124` is byte-identical to `NOTE_COLORS` at `NoteCard.tsx:33`. Grep: `content.color` on a CARD is read by exactly zero frontend components.
- **Failure** — The agent colours nine cards, the review row says "→ amber", the transaction commits, the board looks identical. Worse, it is self-consistent: `digest.go:1045 colorOf` also reads `content.color`, so on the next run the agent **sees its own colours** and believes the taxonomy exists. Only a human eye can detect this.
- **Mechanism** — Compiler: `ActSetColor` writes `backgroundColor` (and its `UndoChanges` twin). Digest: `colorOf` reads `backgroundColor` first, falling back to `color` so pre-existing agent junk still reads. Migration: one sweep moving `color`→`backgroundColor` on agent-origin CARD/CLONE elements.
- **Hard clause** — **probe.** The missing clause is that no existing probe checks a *rendered* outcome, only a compiled one. Needs a frontend render assertion, not another capability test.

#### CV2 · `place_attachment` always creates an IMAGE, so an attached PDF becomes a broken picture 🔴
- **Evidence** — `plan.go:164` `case ActPlaceFile: return domain.TypeImage` unconditionally; `plan.go:799-804` writes `{url, attachmentId, caption}` with no filename/mimeType/size. The composer accepts PDFs (`AgentAsk.tsx:248`); the human path branches on mime (`Toolbar.tsx:118-125`).
- **Failure** — The tool's own comment says "as an image card if it is one, a file card otherwise". The code does not do this. You get `<img src={pdfUrl}>` — a broken image, no filename, no download. It is also unreadable next run: `textFor` returns "(an image — call look_at to see it)" for a document.
- **Mechanism** — Handler: `runPlaceFile` reads the attachment's mimeType and sets `Action.ElementType` explicitly. Compiler: the non-image branch writes the FILE shape `{url, attachmentId, filename, mimeType, size}` — identical to what `uploadFile`+`createOp` writes, so an agent-placed file is indistinguishable from a dropped one.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Type is currently derived from `ActionKind`, a pure function; this needs a per-action override path.

#### CV3 · Connect actions are invisible in the preview — the diagram capability previews as loose cards 🔴
- **Evidence** — `layout.go:186` `if a.Kind == ActConnect { continue }` — connectors never get a Position; `GhostLayer.tsx:39-44` filters on `a.position`, so no connect action ever renders.
- **Failure** — The newest capability wave (design_as flow/tree, connector grammar, relation-typed arrows) is reviewed with the arrows missing. `layout.go:207-212` computes the entire tree geometry **from the edges**, so the one thing explaining why the boxes sit where they do is the one thing the reviewer cannot see. The native product draws a ghost line during a single drag (`LineLayer.tsx:326`); the agent's preview of eight shows none.
- **Mechanism** — GhostLayer gains a second SVG pass resolving each endpoint to either a ghost box (`placed`) or a live element (`boardStore.elements` + `viewStore.sizes`, the same two sources LineLayer uses), drawing a dashed accent arrow. Skip any connector whose endpoints do not both resolve on this canvas — the honesty rule TileBadge already applies.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** ActConnect must carry resolved endpoint ids into the plan projection; today they exist only as FromID/ToID on the staging action.

#### CV4 · Cards inside columns never dim under a proposal — the preview is blind where the agent works 🔴
- **Evidence** — `BoardCanvas.tsx:310` passes `proposed={proposedIds.has(el.id)}` to root-canvas shells; `ColumnView.tsx:85` renders `<ElementShell … inColumn />` with **no** `proposed` prop. `useProposedIds` computes the set correctly.
- **Failure** — M1's blind spot one level down, never closed. A plan moving six cards out of a column, or deleting three inside one, shows nothing: ghosts are suppressed (destination is another canvas), the tile badge only counts things going *in*, and the source cards sit undimmed. Columns are where the agent does most of its filing.
- **Mechanism** — ColumnView subscribes to `useProposedIds()` and forwards per child — three lines. Extend to the Unsorted tray (`UnsortedTray.tsx:107-114` has no proposal awareness at all, and "file my tray" is the single most common request).
- **Hard clause** — **probe.** Trivial code; the acceptance assertion (a moved-out card carries `.agent-proposed`) is what keeps it from regressing.

#### CV5 · Auto-apply never frames the canvas, and no run ever selects what it made 🔴
- **Evidence** — `useAgentShell.ts:44` `if (state !== 'PROPOSED' || !plan) return;` — framing is gated on a state an auto-apply run never enters. `agentStore.ts` contains no call to `select()` anywhere.
- **Failure** — In the mode the composer advertises as "Do it (Enter)", the board changes off-screen: the agent places new work below existing content by design, so a person scrolled elsewhere sees nothing happen and finds strange cards later. And in either mode the new elements are not selected, so every follow-up gesture (Ctrl+D, the floating action bar, "Ask Qomra about these") targets nothing. Every other creation path in the product selects its result.
- **Mechanism** — `useAgentShell` frames on the transition into COMPLETED as well as PROPOSED, keyed on run id so it fires once. `agentStore.apply()` calls `useBoard.getState().select(createdIds)` after the refresh settles — `CreatedIDs` is already a server concept (`plan.go:176`). Guard: skip both when the plan wrote nothing to this canvas.
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (client-side): the ordering between refresh settle and select is the only fragile part.

#### CV6 · Text direction override is a first-class product control the agent cannot touch or see
- **Evidence** — `ElementShell.tsx:254-262` renders an auto/rtl/ltr submenu for 13 element types; `direction.ts:31-38` defines eligibility. `grep -rn textDirection backend/internal/agent` returns nothing.
- **Failure** — On a bilingual product this is not cosmetic: `content.textDirection` also governs Arabic-Indic numeral substitution (`direction.ts:116-136`), so it decides whether numbers in an agent-authored Arabic card render as ٥ or 5. The agent cannot pin an Arabic card RTL, and cannot see that a user pinned one LTR — so a rewrite via `set_note_text` silently inherits whatever auto-detection does to the new first word.
- **Mechanism** — Schema `set_direction(elementId, auto|rtl|ltr)`. Handler: type-gated on TEXT_TYPES, and for CLONE it must target the **source** (`ElementShell.tsx:240-242` does exactly this — clones share content). Compiler: generic content update, `null` for auto. Digest: an `⟨rtl⟩` flag beside the trust label.
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (the clone redirect) and **digest** (without the read, a rewrite still clobbers a human's pin).

#### CV7 · The agent can only write flat paragraphs — and `set_note_text` flattens a human's formatting 🔴
- **Evidence** — `plan.go:854-870 tiptapDoc` splits on `\n` and emits paragraph/text nodes only. The editor loads StarterKit + Link + TextStyle + Color + Highlight + Underline (`NoteCard.tsx:58-66`); the FormatBar offers bold/italic/underline/strike/H1/H2/lists/blockquote/code/link/colour/highlight.
- **Failure** — P4 caught this for table formulas and stopped there. Ask for a treatment and `write_document` produces an undifferentiated wall. The digest compounds it: `textFor` reads only `textPreview`, so the agent cannot see that a document **has** structure — and `set_note_text` ("replaces what is there") will flatten a human's carefully formatted note into paragraphs. That is destructive, not merely limited.
- **Mechanism** — Compiler: a markdown-subset ↔ Tiptap JSON translator (headings, lists, bold/italic/code, links) replacing `tiptapDoc`, with the prompt told the body accepts that subset. Digest: the inverse — render a doc's `doc` JSON back to the same subset instead of `textPreview`, so structure round-trips. Non-negotiable: `set_note_text` on a doc containing marks the translator cannot express must **refuse and say so**.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler**, and specifically the refusal branch. A lossy round-trip that silently succeeds is worse than the current state.

#### CV8 · Selection scope discards the surrounding board, so "put these next to the budget" cannot resolve 🔴 *(deepens U2)*
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:375-378` `case ScopeSelection: if !selected[el.ID] { continue }` — non-selected elements never enter `scope.Elements` or `scope.Items`. `tools.go:1063 resolveExisting` then refuses any id not in scope.
- **Failure** — U2 proposes marking the selection ⭐. The real defect is the opposite: everything *not* selected vanishes. Point at three cards, say "file these under Pre-Production", and the destination board is not in scope — the run fails on an id the person can see on screen. Pointing is the gesture the whole selection scope exists for, and it is the one that breaks. Identical for `ScopeUnsorted`: "file my tray onto the right columns" cannot see the columns.
- **Mechanism** — The scope filter becomes an **attention** filter, not a visibility filter: every eligible element enters `scope.Items`, selected ones flagged ⭐, the rest `⟨context⟩`. Preconditions gain a rule — a plan may READ context items and may only WRITE to starred ones plus containers it creates — with the refusal naming the flag so the model routes rather than retries.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** The read/write asymmetry is a new precondition kind; getting its refusal text right is what makes the model route instead of loop.

#### CV9 · The UI never goes RTL — Arabic renders a translated LTR app, agent surfaces included 🔴 *(deepens U5)*
- **Evidence** — `i18n/index.ts:509-519 setLanguage` sets only `<html lang>`; `settingsStore.ts:63-74 applySideEffects` sets theme/density/dotgrid/shadows/accent and never `dir`; grep for `documentElement.dir` returns nothing. Hardcoded LTR arrows at `AgentDecide.tsx:289` and `AgentDone.tsx:159`.
- **Failure** — U5 addresses the agent's output language. The deeper fact is that full `ar` translations ship into a layout that never mirrors: topbar, tool rail, breadcrumbs, agent bar, review list, outcome card and ghost badges all stay left-to-right. Per-element content is beautifully bidi-aware; the chrome around it is not. For this user, an Arabic agent answering in Arabic inside an unmirrored dock is the single most visible incompleteness.
- **Mechanism** — `applySideEffects` sets `root.dir`; stylesheets migrate to logical properties (the review list already uses `paddingInlineStart` — the precedent exists). Destination arrows become direction-neutral. **The canvas layer keeps physical left/top deliberately** — positions are absolute board coordinates and must not mirror — so the fix is scoped to chrome and explicitly excludes `.canvas-layer`.
- **Hard clause** — none of the five; this is a frontend invariant. Its risk is the exclusion boundary, not a contract.

#### CV10 · Table formulas: the digest shows the formula OR the value, and it currently shows the wrong one 🔴 *(corrects P4)*
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:1152-1172 tableDigest` joins the **raw** cell strings, so a computed cell reads as `=SUM(B2:B9)`; `TableCard.tsx:54-56` displays the **evaluated** value and reveals the formula only on focus. The engine is frontend-only (`lib/formula.ts`).
- **Failure** — P4's mechanism says the digest must render both. Today it renders only the formula, so the agent asked "is the budget over?" has no number at all. It cannot answer, and if it guesses it guesses wrong. This also blocks I2's `compute` tool on exactly the surface where arithmetic matters most.
- **Mechanism** — The formula engine must exist server-side: a Go port of `lib/formula.ts`, kept honest by a **shared fixture table** so the two evaluators cannot drift (`formula.test.ts` already exists). `tableDigest` renders `=SUM(B2:B9) → 4,900`. This is also the cheapest substrate for I2 — once the server can evaluate a cell it can answer sums over columns with no second mechanism.
- **Hard clause** — **digest**, with an unusual constraint: the clause is only safe if the shared-fixture drift test ships with it.

## T2 — collaborator

#### CV11 · The nested board's Unsorted tray is unreachable, and so is the breadcrumb move-up gesture
- **Evidence** — `toolhandlers.go:881` and `:989` gate UNSORTED on `parent == s.scope.Board.ID`; `ElementShell.tsx:132-146` implements drag-onto-breadcrumb → that board's tray.
- **Failure** — Every board has a capture inbox; the agent can write only to the root's. "Park the loose ideas in Pre-Production's tray" cannot be staged, and `file_to_board` always lands on the destination's *canvas* — so "move this up to the project board" dumps a card into the middle of someone's layout instead of their inbox. A1/A2 autonomy is specified around the tray and inherits the limit.
- **Mechanism** — Allow `section=UNSORTED` for any BOARD parent inside the run's containment (both handler sites); give `file_to_board` the same optional `section` enum `move_element` already has. An UNSORTED destination needs no geometry, which *removes* a packing step.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Two gates, both currently comparing against the root id.

#### CV12 · Group-into-column is one human gesture and N+1 agent rows
- **Evidence** — `ElementShell.tsx:263` "Group into column"; `groupIntoColumn` (`:466-479`) commits one transaction of create + N moves. The agent must stage `create_column` then one `move_element` per card, each consuming a `Budget.MaxActions` slot.
- **Failure** — A composite the product treats as atomic costs thirty plan rows, thirty budget slots and thirty lines of review for one decision made with one click. A direct contributor to W4 starvation, and it makes the review unreadable: the reviewer wants to approve "group these under Casting", not scroll thirty identical rows.
- **Mechanism** — `group(elementIds, title)` staged as ONE Action carrying the child list, compiled to the same create+moves op set, rendered as one expandable row, reverted as one unit. The `Copies` machinery on `ActDuplicate` (`plan.go:176-186`) already establishes one-action-owns-many-ids; reuse it.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** One action → many ops with a correct inverse is the pattern to copy, not invent.

#### CV13 · Plan rows can be dropped, retitled, retexted and reparented — never reordered, though order is load-bearing
- **Evidence** — `agentStore.ts:518-524` handles exactly four adjustment kinds; `tools.go:212-215` tells the model "Cards land in the ORDER YOU STAGE THEM".
- **Failure** — The property the prompt declares most load-bearing is the one the reviewer cannot correct. Right cards, right column, wrong sequence means discarding the whole plan and re-asking — precisely the cost per-row adjustment exists to remove. On a pipeline board, sequence IS the meaning.
- **Mechanism** — A fifth adjustment `reindex {seq, before|after: seq}` within a shared parent; `ApplyAdjustments` recomputes fractional indices exactly as `OrderPlan` does, and `computeEffective` mirrors it for the projection. UI: drag handles on `ap-row` within a destination group (the list is already grouped by depth).
- **Hard clause** — **schema.** The adjustment vocabulary is a closed typed set; adding a kind touches server projection and client mirror together.

#### CV14 · Ghosts are the only cards on the canvas you cannot touch
- **Evidence** — `GhostLayer.tsx:229` — a ghost's only pointer handler is `stopPropagation` plus hover. Every real card supports drag, resize, drag-to-connect, context menu and inline edit.
- **Failure** — The canvas is declared the review surface, and on it the agent's output is the only inert object. "That column belongs over there" is a 200ms drag in the product and, in review, a dropdown that lists containers rather than coordinates. The reviewer's judgement about **layout** — the thing they assess instantly and the model cannot — has no input channel.
- **Mechanism** — A `reposition {seq, x, y}` adjustment; dragging a ghost writes it, and the server's layout pass treats a repositioned action as **pinned**, packing the rest around it (`packCanvas` already seeds from occupancy, so a pin is just pre-occupied space).
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** The layout pass must accept a partial pin without abandoning collision-freedom for the rest.

#### CV15 · The review list can be narrowed by destination, never by kind
- **Evidence** — `AgentDecide.tsx:199-208` renders every row unfiltered and unvirtualized; the only narrowing is `tileFocus` (destination board). The counts a filter needs are already computed at `:70-72` (n, created, destructive).
- **Failure** — A 40-action plan is 40 rows in a bar pinned to the bottom of the canvas, and the question a reviewer always has — *what does this delete, and what does it move that I already placed* — requires reading all of them. SearchOverlay has a scope control; TrashPanel splits by actor. The agent's list is the least filterable list in the app and the one where a mistake is most expensive.
- **Mechanism** — Filter chips driven by counts already in hand: All · New · Moves · Deletes, with Deletes selected by default when `destructive > 0` (the warn banner already knows to raise the alarm and then hands over an unfiltered list). Pure frontend composition.
- **Hard clause** — none; this is composition. Listed T2 because it is the precondition for LP3's risk banding.

#### CV16 · Nothing takes the person to the work — a run that fills three sub-boards leaves you looking at three tiles
- **Evidence** — `useAgentShell.ts:60-99 frameProposal` tweens only over actions with a Position on **this** canvas, which by construction excludes everything filed into a nested board; `AgentDone.tsx:60-95` offers Undo/Continue/Retry/Ask-again and no navigation.
- **Failure** — The agent's centre of gravity moved into nested boards (that was the whole W1 wave) and both the review and outcome surfaces stayed on the root canvas. After a plan writing thirty elements into four sub-boards, verifying means opening four boards from memory. TileBadge solves this for the *preview* and evaporates the instant the plan is applied.
- **Mechanism** — The outcome card gains a destination list from the same `tileOf` roll-up GhostLayer already computes — "7 in Pre-Production · 5 in Casting" — each row navigating and selecting what the run wrote there (needs CV5's select). Cheapest honest version: keep tile badges rendered for a few seconds past COMPLETED.
- **Hard clause** — none new; depends on CV5.

#### CV17 · Label attach/detach bypasses the transaction path, so human tagging is not undoable — while the agent's is 🔴
- **Evidence** — `labels.ts:30-48` attach/detach call the REST endpoints then hand-patch `useBoard.setState` directly, never going through `commitTransaction`. The agent's `apply_label` compiles a real op with a full prior-set `UndoChanges` (`plan.go:516-525`).
- **Failure** — Tag three cards by hand, press Ctrl+Z, and the undo reaches *past* the tagging to whatever you did before — silently. Labels also do not broadcast, so a collaborator's chips do not update. The agent is currently the only correct labeller in the app, and the asymmetry will read as an agent bug the first time it bites.
- **Mechanism** — `labels.attach/detach` build an update op on `labelIds` carrying the whole prior array (copy `plan.go:516-519`'s reasoning verbatim — merge-patch replaces wholesale, so the inverse must be the full list) and go through `commitTransaction`. REST stays for label CRUD.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** A product fix that belongs in the agent's wave: "still one undo" is a promise one half of the label surface currently breaks.

#### CV18 · Headings are invisible on re-read, so the agent cannot tell its own landmarks from sticky notes
- **Evidence** — `plan.go:816-819 create_heading` writes a CARD with `variant:"heading"`; `digest.go:623` `case TypeCard, TypeClone: return str("textPreview")` — the variant is never rendered.
- **Failure** — The agent creates landmarks and then cannot see them. A second run reading a board it organised sees "PRE-PRODUCTION" as an ordinary card and will file it into a column, resize it, or reword it. It also cannot honour a heading a human placed — the one structural signal on a freeform board that is not a container.
- **Mechanism** — `Item` gains a Variant field; the digest renders `HEADING id "Pre-Production"` on its own line. Two rules follow free: the filer must not move a heading into a column, and the layout packer treats it as a region label.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** One field; the value is entirely in the two downstream rules it enables.

#### CV19 · `resize` is a write with no matching read
- **Evidence** — `toolResize` takes elementIds + small|medium|large; the digest `Item` struct (`digest.go:60-79`) carries ID/Type/Text/Trust/Labels/Color/Cell/ParentID/Section — no width.
- **Failure** — "Make these the same size" and "give the hero image more presence" are guesses: the agent cannot see that four of five cards are already large. This is exactly the failure the digest's own comment names for colour and labels, repeated for geometry, in the same struct, one field away from the fix.
- **Mechanism** — `Item` gains a Size bucket derived from `location.width` against the same thresholds the resize handler **writes**, so the round-trip is exact; rendered only when it deviates from the default, so it costs nothing on a uniform board.
- **Hard clause** — **digest**, with the exactness constraint (bucket boundaries must be shared with the writer, not duplicated).

#### CV20 · Board tile identity: the agent's colour vocabulary is the wrong vocabulary for a tile *(corrects P7)*
- **Evidence** — `BoardStylePopover.tsx:15-28` — a board's colours are eleven CSS **gradients** plus six flat pastels; `cards.tsx:24-28 tileFor` defaults to a hash-selected gradient; `tools.go:114-124 cardSwatches` are eight note-paper pastels.
- **Failure** — P7 says to serve the icon vocabulary "the way cardSwatches already is". Three problems: cardSwatches is itself broken (CV1); board tiles take gradients, so a flat pastel produces a tile that looks broken beside its siblings; and the icon surface has three mutually exclusive forms (Lucide glyph, letter/number, uploaded image) of which only two are agent-expressible.
- **Mechanism** — Two separately served vocabularies: `boardColors` (the eleven gradients, named) and `boardIcons` (`iconCatalog` names + LETTER_ICONS). `style_board(boardId,{color?,icon?})` validates against them and writes `content.color`/`content.icon` with `iconUrl` nulled, exactly as the popover does. Digest renders a board's current colour and icon so siblings get **distinct** ones rather than randomly colliding.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** Without reading siblings' current styling, distinctness is a coin flip.

#### CV21 · Search reaches the whole account but the plan reaches one subtree, and nothing marks the difference
- **Evidence** — `toolhandlers.go:846` `s.elements.Search(ctx, s.task.Owner, q, 12)` is owner-wide; the returned lines (`id · type · ⟨trust⟩ · text`) look identical to digest lines the agent may freely write to. The human equivalent has an explicit scope toggle.
- **Failure** — The model gets twelve results formatted like its writable working set, some on boards outside containment. It will cite one in a summary, try to move it, and get "there is no element X on this board" — a refusal that is correct and reads as the agent contradicting itself thirty seconds after finding the thing. The boundary is right; its legibility is not.
- **Mechanism** — Label each hit with reachability — `[on this board]` vs `[elsewhere — readable, not writable]` — computed from `scope.Elements` membership, and state the distinction once in system context. This is the ⟨trust⟩ label's own design pattern applied to **authority** instead of provenance, at the cost of one field.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** One field; the win is that the model stops trying and starts routing.

#### CV22 · The agent cannot see the label filter, so "tidy what I'm looking at" is answered against a board the user is not looking at
- **Evidence** — `BoardCanvas.tsx:369-398 LabelFilterBar` toggles `viewStore.labelFilter`; `ElementShell.tsx:51-53` dims non-matching cards; `agentStore.ts:117-128` sends `viewport` and nothing else about view state.
- **Failure** — The filter is a view the person deliberately constructed — "show me only what is Blocked" — and it is invisible to the run. Ask to organise while filtered and the agent reorganises the ninety dimmed cards along with the six lit ones. The precedent is set and half-built: viewport was added for exactly this reason ("the agent was spatially blind").
- **Mechanism** — `TaskSpec` gains `activeLabelIds`; the digest flags matching items ⭐ and adds one line — "the person is currently filtering this board to: Blocked" — enough for the model to scope itself without a hard gate (a filter is a view, not a permission). The composer shows it as a third chip so it is never a hidden input.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** Deliberately soft: a hard gate here would one day refuse the right answer.

## T3 — frontier

#### CV23 · Sketches read as blank and cannot be made or extended 🔴
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:282-295` lists TypeSketch as organizable, but `textFor` has no SKETCH case, so it falls through to `Element.Title()` which reads title/textPreview/filename/url — a sketch carries none.
- **Mechanism** — `textFor` gains `case TypeSketch: return "(a sketch — N strokes)", trustUser` — the minimum that makes it referenceable, matching the IMAGE/FILE treatment that was added for exactly this reason. Creating one is genuinely out of scope for a language model and belongs in `capabilities.cannot`; what must not stand is an element that exists, can be moved, and has no name.

#### CV24 · Quick capture is a global gesture that only works while a panel is open 🔴
- **Evidence** — `UnsortedTray.tsx:47-66` registers the Ctrl/⌘+Enter capture listener **inside the tray component**, so it unmounts whenever the tray is closed; it also uses `window.prompt` rather than the app's own Prompt host. `App.tsx`'s global key map does not include it.
- **Mechanism** — Move the listener into `App.tsx`'s global map and replace `window.prompt` with the in-app host (which is dir-aware and themed; `window.prompt` on an Arabic board is an LTR OS dialog). Then the agent half: the composer's own text should be capturable as a note when the request turns out not to need a run — "just save this as a card" is a frequent outcome that currently costs a discard plus a retype. A2 rules presume a capture path that reliably fires, and the gesture designed to feed it is the least reliable surface in the app.

---

# Corner DA — Scope, data model, and the write path

*Method: every route, service, repository method, the realtime hub, the
delegation/transaction domain, cross-checked against the scope compiler, the
50-tool registry, the handlers and the ops compiler. Three findings were proved
with throwaway Go probes against the in-memory repositories.*

## T1 — parity and live wrongs

#### DA1 · The CLONE update path is a cross-tenant write escalation 🔴 **PROVEN**
- **Evidence** — `transaction_service.go:246-250` (`cloneInstanceWithin` escape in `verifyOpScope`); no validation of `content.cloneSourceId` anywhere at create time. Probe output: `PROBE2 err=<nil>  victim card now="OVERWRITTEN BY MALLORY"`.
- **Failure** — Any authenticated user can overwrite the content of **any element in the database by id**: create a CLONE on your own board whose `cloneSourceId` names a stranger's card, then POST an update op for that card with your own boardId. `ApplyWithMeta` checks `RequireEdit` on the declared board only; `assertWithin` fails for the foreign source, and the synced-note escape hatch returns nil. This is the write-side twin of the CLONE **read** bypass already found and fixed in `BoardService.Children` — the same hole, one layer over, still open.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *a clone may only re-key an edit to a source the caller can already edit.* (1) `cloneInstanceWithin` additionally calls `RequireEdit(ctx, sourceID, p)` — the clone proves **routing**, the ACL proves **authority**, and today only routing is checked. (2) `applyCreate` validates `cloneSourceId` on CLONE creation (must exist, be a CARD, be RequireView-able), mirroring `ElementService.ConvertToClone` which does check both sides.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Delegation already blocks the agent from reaching it, so this is a human-path fix — but it must land before any agent verb stages CLONEs at scale.

#### DA2 · Cross-board filing is structurally impossible — `DestinationBoardIDs` never reaches the write path 🔴 **PROVEN**
- **Evidence** — `transaction_service.go:103` runs `verifyOpScope(op, boardID=run.Task.RootBoardID)` **before** line 110's `verifyDelegation`; `verifyMoveTarget` asserts the new parent is within that one board. Probe with `DestinationBoardIDs=[dest]` → `err=forbidden`. The only coverage is domain-level (`toolchoice_test.go:337` exercises `Roots()`, never a commit).
- **Failure** — An entire subsystem cannot work: `filing.go` (100 lines), `Delegation.DestinationBoardIDs`, `authorizeDestinations`, the `discovered` set, `maxDestinationsPerRun`, the `file_to` tool and the search-marks-discovered plumbing. The verb stages happily, the review shows the destination, and the commit 403s for every destination outside the root subtree — i.e. every case the feature exists for. The two gates were designed as *and*; the first predates delegation and hardcodes the run's root as the IDOR boundary, so the second gate's widening is dead. Silently blocks I5 and A2.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *the IDOR boundary is the set of roots the principal is authorised for, not one boardId.* `ApplyWithMeta` gains a `roots []string`: `[boardID]` for a human (unchanged), `p.Delegation.Roots()` for a delegated principal, with `assertWithinAny`/`verifyMoveTargetAny` replacing the single-board versions. The delegation gate stays an independent second check. The transaction record needs a decision too: today it is stamped with the run's root, so a card filed into B produces a journal entry on A and B's room never hears about it — `MoveAcrossBoards` already solves the multi-room broadcast and the agent path must borrow it.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Two authorization layers whose composition was never tested end to end.

#### DA3 · The staged plan is write-once: every revise verb refuses ids the same run created 🔴
- **Evidence** — `tools.go:1063-1073 resolveExisting` reads only `s.scope.Elements`; contrast `tools.go:987-1005 resolveConnectable`, which explicitly handles `s.created[id]` with a comment saying create-then-relate "is the whole activity". `arrange.go:106` has the same hole. `plan.go:513` hard-errors on labels outside compiled scope.
- **Failure** — ~18 tools go through `resolveExisting` (set_color, set_task_done, assign, set_reminder, caption, collapse, resize, convert, edit_table, set_url, apply_label, duplicate, split, merge, merge_columns, delete, tidy, arrange). None can touch anything the plan just staged. Create 12 cards and tag them all "Q3" — impossible. Create a table then correct a cell — impossible. Create five steps then `arrange` them as a flow — the connectors work (the one exception) and the layout does not. The agent can compose **structure** but not **revision**, which forces every value to be right in the create call and is a hidden cause of the creates-instead-of-edits register failure.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *the plan is a live overlay on the scope, not a write-only log.* `s.resolveTarget(id)` returns a `stagedRef{el, kind, staged}`: scope hit → existing element; `s.created` hit → an element synthesised from the staging Action's own fields. Every call site switches; per-tool type checks read `kind.ElementType()` for staged refs. The compiler mirrors it: for staged targets there is no prior value, so `UndoChanges` is the absent state and the forward change **folds into the create op** where possible — `ActApplyLabel` on a staged note becomes `labelIds` inside the create's changes, which is why DA13 matters.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** The fold-into-create path is where a wrong inverse would silently break revert.

#### DA4 · TASK elements never enter scope — the agent is blind to every checklist item 🔴 **PROVEN** *(also found in the CV corner as three dead verbs)*
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:282-296 organizable` omits `TypeTask`; `admitChildren` skips non-organizable children. Probe: a TASK_LIST "Delivery" containing TASK "Lock the cut" renders as `TASK_LIST · ⟨user⟩ · Delivery` with no children and `scope.Elements[TASK]=false`. `textFor` has no TypeTask case either, and `Element.Title()` does not read `content.text`, so a TASK would render blank even if admitted. `tools.go:1225 readBoard` only enumerates children of a BOARD, never of a TASK_LIST.
- **Failure** — Three shipped tools are structurally unreachable on pre-existing material: `set_task_done`, `assign`, `set_reminder` all call `resolveExisting`, which cannot find a TASK. "Tick off what we finished", "who owns this?", "what is overdue?" are unanswerable by construction, not by model error — and the failure mode is the model inventing an id and getting "there is no element X", which reads as hallucination rather than blindness. P5 (`set_due`) inherits the bug on arrival as a fourth dead verb; A4 (morning digest) reads task state the scope does not contain. `add_tasks` can only ever *append*: the agent writes checklists it can never read back.
- **Mechanism** — Five clauses. Scope: `organizable` gains `TypeTask` (it is a child of a TASK_LIST, and the move guard that motivated exclusion is already handled by `CanHold` in `containment.go:37`). Digest: `textFor` gains a TypeTask case rendering `text` + `[x]/[ ]` + assignee name + due/OVERDUE + ⏰ — one line that unlocks P5's whole reporting register with no new plumbing. Read: `read_board` accepts a TASK_LIST id. Budget: lists get their own `maxPerContainer` (~25) because a half-shown checklist is worse than none.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** The rendering line is the entire feature; the scope admission is two words.

#### DA5 · LINE elements never enter scope — the connector graph is write-only and `edgesAmong` is dead code 🔴 **PROVEN** *(merges the CV corner's connector-styling gap)*
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:486` and `tools.go:1240` both explicitly `continue` on TypeLine. The only consumer, `arrange.go:355-370 edgesAmong`, iterates `scope.Elements` for TypeLine — a set that can never contain one. Probe: a labelled LINE joining two in-scope cards → `edgesAmong(cards) = 0 edges`. On the write side, `LineToolbar` (`LineLayer.tsx:361-416`) exposes colour, start arrow, end arrow, label, dashed, weight and a curve handle; the agent's `connect` schema carries `label` and `relation`.
- **Failure** — `arrange(ids,"flow")` and `arrange(ids,"tree")` — the whole workflow-design capability — silently fall back to edgeless layout on any board that already has a diagram. The agent cannot answer "what depends on what", cannot re-route, relabel, restyle or remove an existing connector, cannot avoid drawing a second arrow between a pair it already joined (spending its 6-connection quota on duplicates), and cannot match a visual convention a human established for arrows. It is also why W8's stranded-connector class had to be swept by a CLI migration rather than noticed: the agent has never been able to see a line.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *a connector is a relationship, not an element to be moved.* Split the two questions `organizable` currently conflates. Keep LINE out of the **movable** set (occupancy must keep ignoring it). Add `scope.Edges []Edge{ID, FromID, ToID, Label, Relation, Canvas}` populated during the same walk from `content.fromId/toId/label`, admitted only when both endpoints resolve inside scope. `edgesAmong` reads `scope.Edges`. The digest renders a terse `LINKS` block (`a → b "leads to"`). New verbs then need no new write plumbing, since `ActConnect`'s compiler branch already writes every key: `relabel_connection`, `disconnect(fromId,toId)`, and optional `{color, weight, dashed, startArrow, endArrow, curve}` constrained to the frontend's own enums.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** The read gap is the serious half; the styling verbs are a schema afternoon once edges exist.

#### DA6 · The collaboration layer is invisible: comments cannot be read, and the one the agent writes always lands on the root board 🔴 *(four corners found this independently)*
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:294-295` excludes COMMENT_THREAD from scope (probe: `scope.Elements[COMMENT_THREAD]=false`); `CommentRepository.ListByThread` has no agent caller; `runComment` (`toolhandlers.go:600-621`) hardcodes `ParentID: s.scope.Board.ID`, and the `comments` quota is 1. `CommentCard` (`cards.tsx:395-507`) renders a full conversation with author names, live socket updates, @mention picker and reactions. The read-only share link explicitly invites viewer comments.
- **Failure** — A whole collaboration surface is one-way. "What did the team decide?", "summarise the open feedback", "reply to Omar's question" are unattemptable — and on a shared board the agent reports the board's *artifacts* while missing its *deliberation*, which is where the decisions live. The `comment` tool can only ever START a thread (`content:{resolved:false}`), so an agent asked to respond opens a second discussion beside the first — the exact create-only failure pattern `add_tasks` and `edit_table` were built to end, still live on the third surface. And because it can only comment on the *board*, its one channel for a caveat is a sticky at the root nobody associates with the card it concerns. The exclusion rationale in the code ("moving it detaches the conversation") is a correct argument against *moving* a thread that was applied to *reading* one.
- **Mechanism** — Same split as DA5. COMMENT_THREAD stays out of the movable set, enters `scope.Elements` for addressability, and renders as an anchored line under its subject: `💬 3 messages on <cardId> — latest: "…" ⟨user⟩ · 👍6 · unresolved` (bodies via `ListByThread`, capped at the 2 most recent, trust-labelled per LP5 — a viewer's comment is `⟨guest⟩`, not `⟨user⟩`). Write: `comment(elementId)` becomes anchorable, `reply(threadId,text)` and `resolve_thread(threadId)` join it, quota rises to ~3 with the refusal reworded to "put the rest in your summary". Routed through `CommentService.Add`, not a raw `Insert` — see MP9.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** Anchoring the thread under its subject (rather than listing threads separately) is what makes the model reason about the argument rather than about a list of conversations.

#### DA7 · `attachPeople` reads only the board's own ACL, so on any sub-board the collaborator list collapses to one person 🔴
- **Evidence** — `service.go:1190-1207` uses `scope.Board.ACL.OwnerID` + `.Editors` only. `AccessResolver.Resolve` (`access.go:44-68`) walks the containment chain and takes the MAX role across every ancestor. `applyCreate` stamps every new BOARD with `ACL{OwnerID: creator, Editors: []}`.
- **Failure** — Sharing cascades downward; the agent's people list does not. On a shared board's sub-board — the normal state after one organizing run, and one the agent itself creates with an empty editor list — `scope.People` contains exactly the creator. `assign` then rejects every real teammate with "X is not one of this board's people", and worse, `rejectID` counts it toward the **injection tally**: asking the agent to assign work to a colleague looks like an attempted prompt injection. Caps P6, whose whole mechanism is resolving @Name against `scope.People`.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *the people who can act on an element are the people the AccessResolver would admit.* `attachPeople` walks upward exactly as `Resolve` does, unioning owner+editors at each board ancestor. The chain is ≤4 deep and already read during scope compile. While there, mark each person's role so `assign` can refuse a view-only visitor with an honest reason.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** The injection-tally interaction is the subtle part: a legitimate name must not increment a security counter.

#### DA8 · Search cannot see content a collaborator created on your own board, and the text index it has is never used 🔴
- **Evidence** — `elements.go:212-227` — the ownership clause is `createdBy == sub OR acl.ownerId == sub OR acl.editors contains sub`; non-board elements carry no ACL (`ACL *ACL` is set only for BOARDs), so a card written by a teammate matches none of the three. The compound text index exists (`indexes.go:23-27`) and `Search` uses a `$regex` collection scan instead.
- **Failure** — `search` is the only read that crosses board boundaries and the sole input to `markDiscovered`, which gates cross-board filing. On any shared board it silently returns a subset: everything the other person wrote is unfindable. "Find everything about the harbour interview" misses half the board and reports "Nothing matches" with total confidence. This is a correctness ceiling **under** I1 — adding embeddings on top of an ownership filter that drops collaborator content just makes the wrong subset rank better.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *search reachability == ACL reachability.* Replace the ownership `$or` with: resolve the caller's reachable board set (`BoardsOwnedBy` already does this) and match `location.parentId` against its transitive closure — or, cheaper and the right long-term shape, denormalise `rootBoardId` onto every element at write time (`applyCreate` and the move path both know it; `SweepOrphanConnectors.loadWithAncestors` is the backfill) and index `{rootBoardId, updatedAt}`. That one field also gives DA21's fingerprint, DA2/DA14's multi-room broadcast, and `CountsByParent` a cheaper query. Switch the match to `$text` so the index earns its keep.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** A migration plus a predicate change; the probe (Bob's card on Alice's board is returned by Alice's search) is a repository contract test.

#### DA9 · `set_reminder` writes `reminderAt` onto any element type, and only TASKs are ever swept 🔴
- **Evidence** — `toolhandlers.go:318-332 runRemind` calls `resolveExisting` with **no type check**; `DueTaskReminders` (`elements.go:263-278`) filters `type: TASK`; the sparse index is `{type, content.reminderAt}`.
- **Failure** — A promise the system cannot keep, made silently. "Remind me about this note next Tuesday" is accepted, appears in the review list as a real change, commits, and never fires — the sweeper cannot see it and the frontend renders `reminderAt` only inside TaskListView. The user gets a confident yes and nothing happens on Tuesday. Exactly the failure shape the capability doctrine names, inside a capability that shipped.
- **Mechanism** — Pick one and make it true. Cheap: `runRemind` refuses non-TASK targets with a **routing** refusal — "reminders live on checklist items; I can add this to a list and set it there" — which composes with DA4. Better: widen the sweep to `content.reminderAt` on any live type, keep the `reminderSent` guard, fall back from `content.text` to `Element.Title()` for the message, and re-shape the index to `{content.reminderAt, content.reminderSent}` sparse.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** The decision is product, the code is four lines either way; what must never ship again is accepted-and-dropped.

#### DA10 · The agent actively disables link previews — and the resolver it needs is already wired into the planner 🔴 *(corrects P2)*
- **Evidence** — `plan.go:781-784` — `ActCreateLink` writes `url`, `title`, `showPreview:false`, `showDescription:false`. `LinkService.Resolve` (`link_service.go:92-130`) returns Title, Description, ThumbnailURL, SiteName, EmbedType and is SSRF-hardened (dials the checked IP). The planner **already holds it** as `links LinkResolver` (`planner.go:361`) and uses it for `read_url`, where it throws ThumbnailURL, SiteName and EmbedType away.
- **Failure** — P2 reads as an omission. It is a decision: the compiler explicitly turns the preview off, so an agent-made link is *worse* than a default one — a grey card that has opted out of being rich.
- **Mechanism** — `runCreateLink` calls `s.links.Resolve` (spending one `urls` unit, degrading gracefully to a bare link on quota exhaustion or fetch failure); the compiler writes `embedType`, `thumbnailUrl`, `description`, `siteName`, `showPreview:true`. The trust label follows the value into the digest, since a fetched title is somebody else's prose. `runReadURL` stops discarding SiteName/EmbedType — "⟨web⟩ YouTube video: …" is what lets the model decide the card should be a playable embed.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** No new plumbing, no new quota decision, no new SSRF surface; the risk is only that the fetched strings enter content unlabelled.

#### DA11 · The delegation's content guard is a two-item denylist over a schemaless map 🔴
- **Evidence** — `transaction_service.go:357-365` — the only forbidden content keys are `isHome` and `isTemplate` (plus `acl`). `Content` is `map[string]any` with no per-type validation.
- **Failure** — The denylist is the wrong shape and the roadmap keeps proving it. `content.locked` is not on it (P1 — a lock the write path does not enforce). `content.cloneSourceId` is not on it (DA1). `content.agentInstructions` is not on it — the standing rules that **constrain the agent** live in freely-patchable content, so the guard against a run rewriting its own instructions is that no tool happens to emit that key today. Every new capability is a fresh chance to forget an entry, and a denylist maintained by everyone who adds a feature will be wrong.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *an agent op may write only the keys its own action kind declares.* Flip to an allowlist derived from the compiler: `plan.go`'s content-writing switch already enumerates, per ActionKind, exactly which keys that action produces. Export it as `ContentKeysFor(kind)`, stamp the originating kind onto the op, and have `verifyDelegation` reject anything outside the set. Privileged keys then need no enumeration anywhere: they are excluded by not being produced. Second clause: per-type shape validation at `applyCreate` (a TABLE needs `cells`, a LINE needs `fromId`/`toId`, a COLOR_SWATCH needs `hex`) closes the human half.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler**, and the table-driven probe that asserts every ActionKind writes only its declared keys — which fails loudly the next time a capability lands without updating its contract.

#### DA12 · Templates: the agent can rewrite your stencils, and `UseTemplate` writes outside the transaction path 🔴 *(corrects P3)*
- **Evidence** — `ElementShell.tsx:289-293` toggles `content.isTemplate`; `grep isTemplate backend/internal/agent` returns nothing. `ElementService.UseTemplate` (`element_service.go:97-158`) copies a template subtree server-side and re-owns each nested BOARD's ACL — with no ops, no `undoChanges`, no `agentRunId`, no broadcast.
- **Failure** — Two halves P3 misses. `isTemplate` is invisible to the scope walk, so a template board nested in the workspace is admitted as ordinary content (it is a BOARD, therefore organizable) and "organise this board" will happily reorganise your blank sprint template, rename its columns, or fill it in. A template is a stencil; writing into it is destructive in a way nothing prevents. And the agent cannot simply *call* `UseTemplate`: instantiation would be invisible to the ghost preview, absent from the review list, unrevertible by per-run revert, and unseen by other clients until refresh — the same shape as DA14, a legitimate service method that predates the transaction discipline.
- **Mechanism** — Before the verbs: the scope walk flags `isTemplate` boards, Preconditions refuses **writes** into them with a refusal that names why, while still permitting **reads** (reading a template is how the agent learns your conventions — P3's actual value). Digest renders `TEMPLATE b7 "Sprint planning"`. Then `UseTemplate` gains a `Plan(...) ([]CopySpec, error)` sibling that walks the subtree and assigns ids **without writing** — precisely what `staging.planCopies` already does for `duplicate`, including the deterministic `copyID` derivation that makes a retried apply idempotent. `use_template` stages an `ActDuplicate`-shaped action over those specs; the existing compiler branch emits the creates; `maxCopyElements` (60) bounds it; ACL re-ownership becomes a compiler concern.
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (the write refusal) and **compiler** (the plan-without-writing split). The verb is the easy part.

## T2 — collaborator

#### DA13 · `applyCreate` silently discards `labelIds` and `acl` from a create op 🔴
- **Evidence** — `transaction_service.go:640-652` — the constructed Element copies only `type`, `location`, `content`; `labelIds` and `acl` are dropped without error.
- **Failure** — A create op is not a full element write, and nothing says so. Any client (and the agent, once DA3 folds labels into creates) gets a 201 and an untagged element. Same silent-drop class as the `content.cells`/`content.hex` bugs the capability wave already fixed, and it means create and update have different undocumented field sets — exactly the asymmetry a compiler branch gets wrong.
- **Mechanism** — `applyCreate` accepts `labelIds` (validated as the caller's own, reusing `LabelService.Attach`'s ownership check, incrementing `usageCount`) and **rejects** an op carrying `acl` with ErrValidation rather than ignoring it — sharing is not a create-time concern and a silently-ignored ACL is worse than a refused one.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** The refusal-vs-ignore decision is the whole point.

#### DA14 · `MoveAcrossBoards` bypasses the delegation gate and writes an unattributed transaction 🔴
- **Evidence** — `transaction_service.go:471-546` patches elements directly via `MergePatch`, never calls `verifyOpScope` or `verifyDelegation`, and builds a transaction with no `Origin` and no `AgentRunID`.
- **Failure** — (a) The audit trail lies: every drag-onto-a-breadcrumb produces a transaction whose Origin is `""`, which the frontend reads as "absent = human" — accidentally correct today and wrong the moment anything else calls this method. (b) It is a standing authority hole for the roadmap: the only service method that legitimately crosses board boundaries is also the only write path with no delegation check, so the natural implementation of DA2, X1 or A2 — "just call MoveAcrossBoards" — hands the agent unattenuated cross-board reach in one line. `Apply`'s own doc comment says "there is deliberately no separate agent write path"; this is one.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *one write path, one gate.* Compose ops and delegate to `ApplyWithMeta` with DA2's roots widening. It already computes correct forward/undo pairs and already handles the multi-room broadcast — that broadcast moves **into** `ApplyWithMeta` as `broadcastToSourceBoards(moved)`, which DA2 needs anyway. Stamp `Origin: OriginHuman` explicitly so "empty origin" stops being a meaningful state.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Deleting a bespoke path is the fix; the risk is the broadcast semantics it currently owns.

#### DA15 · The transaction journal is never deleted — not on trash purge, not on board delete, not on account deletion 🔴
- **Evidence** — `TransactionRepository` is Insert/Get/ListByBoard with **no delete method of any kind**. `AccountService.DeleteAccount` hard-deletes elements, labels, attachments, notifications and calls every `TenantPurger` — and never touches `transactions`. `PurgeExpired` deletes elements only.
- **Failure** — Two problems in one absence. **Privacy:** `Op.Changes`/`Op.UndoChanges` are full content snapshots (textPreview, table cells, document bodies), so "delete my account" leaves a complete queryable copy of everything the user ever wrote, keyed by their userId. The product has a privacy tab and a data-export endpoint; this is the one collection that ignores both. **Growth:** the highest-write collection in the system has no retention, while the elements it describes are purged at 90 days — so most journal rows eventually describe elements that no longer exist, degrading exactly the revert and audit features that make runs trustworthy.
- **Mechanism** — `DeleteByUser(ctx, sub)` and `DeleteOlderThan(ctx, cutoff)`; wire the first into `DeleteAccount`, the second into `MaintenanceService.SweepOnce` and `migrate`, with a retention window **longer than TrashRetention** so a restore can still find its inverse (180 days is the honest number). Index `{createdAt}` so the sweep is a range delete.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** A repository contract test is the only thing that will keep the next purge path from forgetting it again.

#### DA16 · The board tile stats aggregation is unreached — the agent has no cheap way to size a sub-board
- **Evidence** — `CountsByParent` → `BoardService.ChildBoardStats` → `GET /boards/:id/childstats`. One `$group` returning per-parent, per-type live counts. No agent caller.
- **Failure** — The scope walk elides aggressively, and the only way to learn what is inside an elided board is `read_board` — a model step plus a context flood. A one-call answer to "how big is each of these five boards, and of what" already exists and is what the **human's** board tiles are built from: the agent navigates a workspace whose own subtitles it cannot read. It is also the cheapest fix for elision honesty: `… 12 more` becomes `… 12 more (9 cards, 3 boards)`.
- **Mechanism** — Digest clause, no new tool: during `CompileScope`, after the frontier is fixed, call `CountsByParent` once with every container id and fold the result into the elision note and each BOARD's line — `BOARD b1 "Pre-Production" — 9 columns, 31 cards`, which is exactly the string W1's mechanism specified and which is currently computed by counting whatever happened to be admitted.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** One call, and it makes W1's stated acceptance line actually true.

#### DA17 · The clone fan-out means one edit can rewrite N boards, and the agent can neither see it coming nor warn
- **Evidence** — `transaction_service.go:426-448 fanOutCloneUpdates` re-broadcasts every update op to every other board holding a CLONE of the edited card. `CloneInstances` — which returns instances **with their board titles** — has no agent caller. `textFor` groups CLONE with CARD.
- **Failure** — `set_note_text` on a synced card silently changes it everywhere, potentially on boards outside the delegation root, since the edit is at the source. The review says "edit note X" and shows one row; the true blast radius is N boards. This is the only place where an approved change has an effect the review cannot describe — precisely the property the staged-preview architecture exists to prevent.
- **Mechanism** — Two reads. Digest: a card with live instances renders `🔗 also on "Casting", "Ep 2"` — one `CloneInstances` call per **edited** card at staging time, not for the board. Review: `ActSetText`/`ActRename` on a synced card carries a warning the outcome card surfaces, and the postcondition check treats those boards as touched.
- **Hard clause** — **digest**, scoped narrowly: per-edited-card, never per-board, or the scope compile gets an N+1.

#### DA18 · Trash restore is reachable, batch-correct, and the agent still cannot name it *(deepens P8; merges the CV corner's two-bucket finding)*
- **Evidence** — `ActionRestore` is a first-class op the write path handles (`transaction_service.go:605-614`, restoring the whole `trashBatchId`), `CapabilityForAction` maps it, `RestoreBatch` exists, `Trashed(ownerSub)` scopes to `deletedBy == sub OR createdBy == sub` — and no agent tool emits it. The panel (`TrashPanel.tsx:47-48`) splits on `deletedByMe`.
- **Failure** — P8 is *smaller* than it looks and its constraint is different. (1) The op, the capability mapping and the batch semantics all ship — this is schema + handler + digest with no write-path work. (2) `Trashed`'s predicate means an agent restore reaches exactly what the person could restore by hand. (3) The `trashBatchId` grouping means "bring back the casting column" restores the column **and its cards** as one unit — the reason the feature reads as magic, and the reason a naive per-element restore verb would be wrong. But P8's "owner-scoped" is wrong: the model is per-**actor**, so an agent listing across both buckets can silently resurrect a colleague's deliberate cleanup.
- **Mechanism** — `list_trash` returns only the delegating human's own entries, each annotated with its batch size — "restoring this brings back 7 items" — so the model states the blast radius before proposing. `restore(elementId)` stages `ActRestore`; the review row must state the batch count, because "restore 1 thing" that returns 13 is the same unreviewable surprise as DA17. Preview-gated regardless of autonomy.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** The batch-size annotation is what makes the verb honest.

#### DA19 · Unsorted is a first-class tray in the schema and a second-class citizen in the agent
- **Evidence** — `SectionUnsorted`, `GET /boards/:id/unsorted` and `BoardService.Unsorted` treat the tray as its own ordered list. In the agent it renders only as a `[unsorted]` suffix on an item line (`tools.go:1249`), never as a section with its own ordering, and `arrange` refuses it ("in the tray, which is a list and has no positions").
- **Failure** — The tray is the capture inbox — the highest-value thing to ask an agent to process, and the destination `file_to` writes into. In the digest it is an adjective on a scattered set of lines rather than a queue with a length and an order, so "file my inbox" gives the model no sense of how much there is, and "file the oldest ten" cannot be expressed. `DetectDrift` measures canvas clutter and skips the tray entirely, so the one board state that most obviously wants an agent never triggers the ambient suggestion.
- **Mechanism** — Render `UNSORTED (7 items, oldest first)` as its own block above ITEMS, in `location.index` order, **with the count stated even when the contents are elided** — a queue's length is the fact that decides whether to act. `DetectDrift` gains a tray rule, which is a truer drift signal than density and directly feeds U6. `arrange`'s refusal stays correct but gains its complement: `reorder(ids)` over an ordered container, which `OrderPlan` already computes for columns.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** Stating the count under elision is the non-obvious half.

#### DA20 · Fingerprint granularity: one membership hash over the whole subtree invalidates every pending plan 🔴 *(merges the MP corner's mid-run-edit finding)*
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:238-268` adds `__members` = `hashIDs(every id in scope.Elements)`; `CheckMembership` returns `ErrStalePlan` on any difference. After W1, that hash covers the entire workspace to depth 4 / 400 elements.
- **Failure** — The per-element half is carefully scoped ("a collaborator editing an untouched card elsewhere must not invalidate a pending preview") and the membership half throws that away: any create or delete **anywhere** in the subtree invalidates a plan that touches none of it — a colleague adding one card three boards down, or the reminder sweeper stamping `reminderSent`. AGENT_PERFECTION records this firing once for real on deploy. On a two-person board a 30-action plan is nearly un-appliable, and it makes A1/A2 unshippable: a scheduled run and a person working at the same time starve each other.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *membership is per-destination, not per-workspace.* Replace the single hash with `__members:<parentID>` for each distinct parent the plan writes into, hashing only that container's live child id-set. The property that motivated the hash — a new card orphaned outside a grouping built without it — is preserved exactly where it matters and dropped where it never mattered. Second clause: when a fingerprint check *does* fire, use the stale-id list it already returns to **drop those actions and keep the rest**, surfacing the drop as an honest `unmet` ("I did not touch the Casting card — Sara edited it while you were reviewing") rather than aborting the plan. Staleness becomes reportable: "Casting gained an item while you were reviewing".
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Per-action rebase reuses the ApplyAdjustments path, so the risk is entirely in getting the partition of the hash right.

#### DA21 · No idempotency on the write path, and the agent's pinned transaction id fails open
- **Evidence** — `POST /api/v1/transactions` accepts no idempotency key; `TransactionRepo.Insert` returns the raw driver error and never maps duplicate-key to `ErrConflict` (unlike `ElementRepo.Insert`, which does). The agent pins `TxnID: run.ID` deliberately, and `ApplyWithMeta` applies every op **before** inserting the journal row.
- **Failure** — A retried commit — network blip, `RetryAgentRun`, a double-click that beats the state transition — re-runs every op and only then hits the duplicate `_id`. Creates fail mid-loop on element duplicate-key, leaving a partial write with no journal row and no inverse; moves and updates are re-applied idempotently by luck of MergePatch. The run state machine is the only thing preventing this, which makes durability a property of an in-process goroutine — and blocks A1/A2 outright, since a scheduled run has no human to not-double-click.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *the journal row is the commit record, so it is written first.* Insert the transaction in a `created` state before applying, map duplicate-key to `ErrConflict` and return the **existing** transaction — true idempotency, where a retry observes the first commit rather than repeating it. Accept an optional `Idempotency-Key` header for human clients.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Reordering write-then-journal to journal-then-write is small and touches the most load-bearing method in the system.

#### DA22 · Nothing consumes the transaction stream server-side — the hook A2/A3 need does not exist
- **Evidence** — `domain.TransactionBroadcaster` has exactly one implementation, `realtime.Hub`, in-process and fire-and-forget; `ApplyWithMeta` calls it and then hand-rolls `fanOutCloneUpdates` and `notifyAssignments` as bespoke post-commit side effects.
- **Failure** — A2 ("when something lands in Unsorted, file it"), A3 ("tell me when the budget column changes") and I1's index refresh (which explicitly says "refreshed on write via the existing transaction broadcast path" — that path is a WebSocket fan-out, not a subscription bus) all need a place to hang a server-side consumer and there is none. Each would arrive as a fourth hand-rolled call, which is how that method becomes the god object the architecture wave just finished splitting. The single-process assumption also bounds the deployment: two replicas mean two hubs and rooms that cannot see each other.
- **Mechanism** — Invariant: *committing is publishing.* `domain.TransactionSubscriber { OnCommitted(ctx, *Transaction) }` and a small in-process bus that `ApplyWithMeta` publishes to once, after the journal insert. Hub, clone fan-out, assignment notifier and later rules/watchers/embeddings all become subscribers; the method drops to publish-and-return. Subscribers run in a bounded pool with per-subscriber panic recovery, because **a rule must never be able to fail a human's drag.**
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** The interface is the seam a real broker slots into later; the in-process implementation is the honest fit today.

#### DA23 · No transaction size cap for humans, and the containment walk is a query per op per level
- **Evidence** — Op count is bounded only for delegated principals (`d.MaxOps`); the human path's only limit is `BodyLimit("64M")`. `withinBoard` memoises on `elementID|boardID`, so N distinct elements produce N independent ancestor walks, each a `Get` per level; `verifyDelegation` walks again per root; `assertNoCycle` a third time per move. `Descendants` has no depth or size cap at all.
- **Failure** — The delegation op budget is `MaxActions*4 = 240`, so a 240-op plan over a 4-deep workspace issues on the order of a thousand serial round-trips inside one request before a byte is written — the real ceiling on plan size is latency, not the budget number, and W4's "raise MaxSteps so big jobs finish" runs into a wall nothing measures. On the human side there is no ceiling at all. Unbounded `Descendants` is the same hazard on the read side, in `Duplicate`, `Export`, `DeleteAccount` and the delete cascade.
- **Mechanism** — Three bounds and one shared walk. (a) A server-wide `maxOpsPerTransaction` (~500) for every principal. (b) Replace three independent walks with one `ancestryOf(ids)` batch resolve per transaction — the level-at-a-time `$in` pattern `SweepOrphanConnectors.loadWithAncestors` already implements, turning O(ops × depth) into O(depth). (c) `Descendants` takes a limit and returns a truncation flag so callers refuse rather than swallow.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** The probe is a query-count benchmark, which the suite does not currently know how to assert.

#### DA24 · Sharing state is unreadable by the agent, so it cannot reason about who sees what
- **Evidence** — `ShareService.State` is owner-gated with no agent path; ACL editors, `PublicEditLink` and `ViewLink` reach the agent only as the raw sub list `attachPeople` derives; `aclHash` treats the ACL as an opaque postcondition token.
- **Failure** — The agent commits to boards without knowing they are published. "Draft the client-facing summary" on a board carrying a live public view link should read differently from the same request on a private board. It cannot warn that a card it is writing is world-readable, cannot answer "who can see this?", and cannot honour "don't put that where the client will see it". It must never *change* sharing (delegation forbids `acl` outright, correctly) — the inability to *read* it is an accident of the same guard.
- **Mechanism** — Read-only digest clause, no verb. One line: `SHARING: private` / `SHARING: 2 editors` / `SHARING: ⚠ public view link — anything on this board is world-readable`, resolved by walking ancestors so a sub-board inherits exposure honestly. Prompt gains the register rule: a public board raises the bar on what gets written, and the run says so in its summary. `aclHash` unchanged.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** Reading exposure while remaining unable to alter it is exactly the shape wanted.

#### DA25 · Delegation cannot express scope-by-type, rate, or time-of-day — the vocabulary autonomy needs
- **Evidence** — `delegation.go:20-60` — a grant is `{RunID, OnBehalfOf, RootBoardID, Capabilities[4], Consequence, MaxOps, DestinationBoardIDs, ExpiresAt}`. `Capability` is four coarse verbs; `CapabilityForAction` maps Restore onto Delete.
- **Failure** — Every attenuation the autonomy quarter needs is inexpressible. A2 wants "may update the element that triggered this and nothing else" — there is no element-level scope. A3 wants a truly read-only grant — `ConsequenceRead` exists in the enum and no code path ever mints it. A1 wants "only between 02:00 and 04:00, at most once a day" — `ExpiresAt` is a single instant with no window and no rate. Nothing can say "may edit text but may not move things", the natural grant for a copy-editing run. And Restore sharing `CapElementDelete` makes a restore-only grant impossible, blocking DA18's safe shape. Well-built for the one workload it has; no room for the next four.
- **Mechanism** — Widen the grant, keeping every existing field's meaning. Add `element.restore` (split from delete). Add `TargetIDs []string` — a closed element-level allowlist, empty meaning "the whole root" — checked in `verifyDelegation` beside the containment walk; A2's per-element blast radius becomes one line. Add `Types []ElementType`. Add `NotBefore` beside `ExpiresAt`. Keep the rule that everything is a **narrowing**. Add `Delegation.Describe()` rendering the grant in one human sentence for the review card, because a grant nobody can read is a grant nobody is consenting to.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Four new axes, each of which must fail closed.

## T3 — frontier

#### DA26 · Labels are create-and-apply only: no rename, no merge, no retire, and `usageCount` is never read
- **Evidence** — `LabelService.Update`/`Delete` and `Label.UsageCount` (maintained by `IncrementUsage`) have no agent path; `attachLabels` puts only `{ID, Name}` into scope.
