```
CG5 (memory store) ─────────────▶ CG6 decay ─▶ LP1 enforcement ─▶ IN14 suggestion ─▶ LP15 playbooks
MP6 (public/private run split) ─▶ MP7 memory, MP10 notifications, MP17 reclaim, LP10 inbox
DA2 (cross-board write) ────────▶ HORIZON I5, A2, PL19 clipper, CV11 tray filing
DA22 (subscriber bus) ──────────▶ A2 rules, A3 watchers, I1 index refresh, IN6 facilitation
DA20 (per-destination hash) ────▶ A1/A2 (background runs racing humans starve each other otherwise)
DA25 (delegation vocabulary) ───▶ LP9 policy, DA18 restore-only grant, A3 read-only grant, MP18
MP10 (notifications) ───────────▶ A1, A3, A4 — all three are *defined* as "lands as a notification"
PL6 (mid-loop budget) ──────────▶ any unattended run; LP14 fan-out shares the accumulator
PL1 + PL2 ──────────────────────▶ PL7, PL17, PL19, PL20, PL21 (no credential, no ecosystem)
PL9 (tenant query) ─────────────▶ PL6, PL15, LP4, LP10
DA8 (rootBoardId denormalisation) ▶ DA2 broadcast, DA20 fingerprint, MP5 lock, I1 search quality
I1 (embeddings) ────────────────▶ IN1 clustering, CG12 relevance — its only two real consumers
CG14 (variants) ────────────────▶ LP13 (disagreement IS the uncertainty signal)
CG16 (raster) ──────────────────▶ the honest version of M1's nested preview
LP3 (risk banding) ─────────────▶ any autonomy above one card; also LP10 triage, LP16 voice apply
DA3 (staged revision) ──────────▶ IN4 classify, IN1 clustering follow-ups, most composites
```

## Product decisions, not engineering

These need an answer from the product before anyone estimates them. Each is
listed with the choice, not a recommendation.

- **MP1 · Who may run the agent on a shared board.** Owner-only, editors, or link editors? And who pays — runner or owner? Everything in the multiplayer corner hangs off this one record.
- **DA9 · Reminders on non-tasks.** Refuse with a routing message, or widen the sweep to any element? Both are four lines; only one is a product promise.
- **IN6 · Facilitation as a product direction.** Votes and timers are new element types and a new run mode. High ceiling, real surface area, and it changes what QomraNote *is*.
- **IN18 / X5 · Generated content.** Widgets and imagery raise cost, moderation and identity questions before engineering ones. HORIZON already parked X5 deliberately; IN18 belongs in the same conversation.
- **PL6 · Credits, visibly, or caps, silently.** A visible balance is a pricing decision. A silent cap that refuses is a support ticket.
- **LP9 · How much autonomy is offered at all.** The policy record is easy; deciding what a person may configure is not. The one engineering invariant to hold regardless: delete is never in an unattended policy.
- **PL13 · Whether teams exist.** A workspace object is cheap now and expensive after three features assume its absence.
- **PL17 · Opening the board to external agents.** The strongest strategic move available and a decision about what the product is for. The propose-only grant is what makes it safe; shipping without it would trade away the product's best property.
- **PL20 · Email in and out.** The settings dialog currently promises outbound email the product does not send. Either wire it or stop promising it.
- **CV9 · RTL chrome.** Mirroring the whole application is a design project, not a patch, and the canvas layer must be excluded deliberately.

## What this document does not cover

Two things were deliberately excluded so nobody re-derives them. **Computer-use /
the agent driving its own UI:** the typed write path is strictly better in every
respect, and the only interesting inversion of it is CG16 (let the model *see*
the canvas, not operate it). **Generative-UI protocols (AG-UI, A2UI):** the ghost
layer already is the declarative tier that literature recommends, built on a
typed plan the server compiles — the product arrived there first by a different
road.

---

*The five-clause rule remains the law of every wave: schema, handler, compiler,
digest, nested-workspace probe — plus the amendment this sweep earned, that a
probe must assert a rendered result and not merely a compiled op. A capability
whose write lands on a key nothing reads is not a feature. It is a lie the agent
then reads back and believes.*

---

---

# PART TWO — Sweep two: the eight territories the first fleet could not reach

*2026-07-31, same day, second fleet. Eight researchers, eight corners chosen for
one property: each one requires **leaving an assumption** the first three
documents shared. Sweep one — PERFECTION (W1–W10, M1–M5), HORIZON (P/I/U/A/X/S)
and Part One above (145 items, CV/DA/IN/LP/CG/MP/PL) — examined one run, on one
board, at one moment, through the eyes of a sighted mouse user on a desktop with
a fast connection and a board small enough to fit in a digest. Every finding
below comes from breaking exactly one of those: another input modality (AX),
another device (MO), another order of magnitude (SC), another point in time (IL,
JN, FR), another trade (DF), another jurisdiction over the same bytes (DL).*

*214 new items — 119 T1, 74 T2, 21 T3, with 98 marked as confirmed live
wrongs — on a codebase three sweeps had already walked.
The honest reading of that number is not that the first three documents were
shallow. It is that they were **narrow** — and that the narrowness was invisible
from inside, because every researcher shared it.*

---

## What sweep two reveals that sweep one could not

**1. The agent has been polished to a standard the product around it never
reached.** This is the single most repeated pattern, found independently by five
territories. The agent bar is the only surface in the product with a width media
query — four exist in 2,405 lines of CSS and three are the agent's (AX25). The
agent's panels are the only fully translated ones; `BoardsDrawer`, `ShareDialog`,
`TrashPanel`, `TemplatePicker`, `SearchOverlay` and `NotificationsBell` contain
zero `t(...)` calls between them, and the rail button that opens the agent is the
only rail button whose label is hardcoded English (JN4). The agent is already the
only correct labeller in the app (CV17, sweep one). The agent's digest is the
only place in the entire system where a board is described in the spatial
language a person would use — and it is spoken exclusively to a language model
(AX35). **Several waves' worth of the highest-leverage work in this document is
not agent work at all.** It is the product catching up to its own agent.

**2. The code's own comments are the best defect detector in the repository.**
Seven times, a doc comment states a property with precision and the code beneath
it does not have that property — and in every case the comment is *why nobody
looked*. `digest.go:442` says stable ordering "is what lets the prompt cache hit
at all"; nothing in `Messages` is ever marked cacheable (SC1). `resilient.go:27`
says `lastUsed` exists so "a quality regression can be explained later"; nothing
reads it and it races (IL9). `hub.go:1` says "concurrency is element-granular …
last writer wins"; `MergePatch` ends in an unpredicated `ReplaceOne`, so it is
document-granular and two concurrent patches discard each other whole (SC7).
`transaction_service.go:96` says "a transaction is all-or-nothing in intent, so
we never apply op 1 then fail on op 2"; the pre-validation loop it describes
cannot pre-empt a write failure, and there is no rollback (FR5). `r2.go:70` says
"the client re-resolves stale attachment URLs on 403"; that route does not exist
and no client code attempts it (DL3). `contracts.go:216` reasons the deadline was
raised 3→8 minutes so `MaxSteps` could bind; a 5-minute reconciler kills the run
first (FR1). And `contracts.go:368` documents the 30-minute-grant /
2-hour-proposal mismatch **in prose**, as a known defect, and it shipped (FR12).

**3. The measurement instrument has four more blind axes than sweep one found.**
M5 said the corpus never leaves flatland; Part One added that it never leaves
single-player (MP11) and never runs a probe twice (CG4). Sweep two adds four
more, each proven by a grep: it never leaves **the small** (largest fixture is 12
elements against a 400-element scope ceiling and a 240-op delegation cap; `grep
-rn "func Benchmark" backend/` returns zero — SC22); it never leaves **the
un-delegated** (the harness calls the planner directly and commits with a bare
`&domain.Principal{Sub:"eval"}` carrying no `Delegation`, so the entire
authorization envelope is unmeasured — IL17); it never leaves **the sighted** (a
real-DOM render harness for every agent surface exists and asserts nothing about
perceivability — AX33); and it never leaves **the domain-ungraded** (fourteen of
thirty-three probes are film-shaped and all route to a grader that counts
containers — DF36). Seven axes on one instrument, four of them new.

**4. The product's most consequential promises are the ones nothing keeps.** Not
agent promises — product ones, in the confirmation dialogs for irreversible
actions. "This permanently deletes … all uploaded files": no code path removes a
blob (DL1). "Delete forever" on a card: the bytes stay, publicly fetchable, on an
unauthenticated route (DL2). "The board is back as it was" after a revert:
connector rehoming and assignment notifications are outside the invertible set
(FR19). "Nothing was changed" after cancelling mid-apply: forty elements just
landed (FR3). "Remove" in the share dialog: the removed person keeps receiving
the full content stream until their token expires (DL11). And "Download my data —
everything you own": no trash, no files, no comments, no agent history, nothing
from shared boards (DL13).

**5. And the inversion that reframes the roadmap.** A person who cannot drag a
card can *ask* for the board to be organised. That is a rare and genuine form of
access — better than any keyboard emulation of dragging, because it operates at
the level of intent rather than gesture. On a phone the same argument holds for a
different reason: direct manipulation degrades roughly with the square of the
screen shrink, while asking is scale-invariant. The substrate is already
built — 50 typed verbs, staged writes, one-transaction commit, per-action
revert, delegation attenuation. What blocks the claim is a small, enumerable set:
the capabilities reachable by **neither** pointer-free input **nor** the agent —
lock, text direction, template conversion, sketch, group-into-column (AX24).
Closing that set is the acceptance criterion for the whole inversion, and it
contains the product's protection primitive and its bilingual primitive.

---

## Corrections to the prior documents — read before planning any wave

Twenty-one. Six would send a wave in the wrong direction; three are live product
lies; one (LP1) is losing recoverable data every hour the product runs.

| Prior item | Claim | Correction | Item |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LP1** | Detect stage: "at commit, diff applied vs proposed" | **Impossible retroactively and wrong on its commonest shape.** `service.go:574` `run.Plan = effective` overwrites the proposal at commit, so every run already applied has lost the thing it was adjusted from — and `Adjustment` (`contracts.go:276-281`) carries only `json` tags, no `bson`, so it has no persistent home at all. Separately, cascade drops and human drops share one `dropped` map (`verify.go:376,404-417`), so one click dropping a column of nine writes ten correction records. | IL1, IL2 |
| **LP4** | "actions-per-turn — W4's starvation metric, measured in production" is among four things a new RunOutcome record would enable | **Already emitted on every turn** — `planner.go:506-509` `{"step":n,"calls":len}`. So are the proposed count (`EvPlanReady`) and applied count (`EvOpsCommitted`). The journal is not missing data; it is missing an index and a query shape. Separately `revertedWithin` stays uncomputable even after LP4 lands, because `transition` writes `CompletedAt` for **every** terminal state and COMPLETED→REVERTED overwrites the apply time. | IL25, IL5 |
| **LP4** | Hard clause: "the promoted probe must run in the existing harness unmodified" | **Unsatisfiable.** `agenteval.go:211` calls `agent.NewPlanner(...).Run(...)` directly; `agent.Service`, the RunStore, the state machine, Apply, adjustments, Discard and Revert are never constructed. No probe can express "applied with adjustments", which is the defining property of a promoted correction. | IL17 |
| **PL15** | "a refusal histogram from event types" | **There are no refusal event types.** `reply.fail` (`tools.go:1201-1216`) returns to the model with no emit; the transcript is a local slice that dies with the function. And `EvRunCreated` is emitted for admission, refinement **and** steering, so the first histogram anyone builds double-counts runs. | IL26, IL8 |
| **PL6** | "an invisible meter that silently refuses is the worst possible failure mode" | **The meter exists and is good.** `AgentAsk.tsx:301-311` renders remaining daily balance with a `low` class at 80%, plus the per-pass ceiling and pass multiplier. The real gaps are elsewhere: mid-run spend is hidden for the whole run, the cap is admission-time only so K concurrent runs all pass against a stale sum, and `cache_creation_input_tokens` is never read so every run's cost is under-reported by its own cache write. | FR24, SC15, SC2 |
| **PL4** | Share target / file handlers / shortcuts, T1 "because the PWA already exists" | **On iOS it does not meaningfully exist**: no `scope`, `id`, `lang`, `dir`, maskable or raster icon in the manifest; no `apple-touch-icon`, no `apple-mobile-web-app-*` metas in `index.html`. And PL4's POST share target has no receiver — `sw.js:27` returns early on any non-GET. PL4 is blocked behind MO10/MO11 and cannot be T1. | MO10, MO18 |
| **I3** | "the film-production user asks for this constantly in disguise: shoot schedules ARE timelines" | **They are the opposite.** A timeline lays items along their dates; a stripboard *assigns* dates by solving location/cast/day-night constraints — the dates are the output. Story order is the one order a shooting schedule is never in. Built as specified, I3 would arrange scenes 1..64 along an axis, which is the script. | DF12 |
| **M3** | "`set_note_text` accepts DOCUMENT targets … digest already shows document bodies, so the model can read-then-revise" | **It shows 200 characters** (`digest.go:30,605`), and `read_board` — the tool for seeing *more* — truncates at 100 (`tools.go:1248`). `set_note_text` then replaces `textPreview` **and** `doc` wholesale. "Tighten the second act" reads 200 characters and deletes six pages, and the review row shows only what will exist. | JN6 |
| **U6** | "the ambient-suggestion machinery exists; surface it in the empty-composer state" | **Nothing to surface.** `drift.go:50-52` returns nil on its first line for a board with zero items, and all four thresholds describe weeks of use. The three static fallback presets include "organize into columns" and "summarize this board" — two no-ops on an empty board. | JN2 |
| **CV9** | Mechanism: "stylesheets migrate to logical properties" | **Necessary and insufficient.** Nine GSAP animations move panels in physical pixels (`BoardsDrawer.tsx:29` `x:-340`, `TrashPanel.tsx:23`/`UnsortedTray.tsx:19` `x:340`) and two inline computations set `paddingLeft`/`marginLeft` for task nesting. Ship CV9 alone and you get a mirrored app with three panels sliding in from the wrong side. | AX28 |
| **IN15** | Caption sweep, framed as "as much a perception fix as an accessibility one" | **It undersells itself.** `cards.tsx:214` already wires `alt={c.caption ?? ''}`, so `caption_images()` is not an accessibility *lens* — it is the alt-text pipeline, finished except for the batch call. On a filmmaker's reference board, `alt=""` on forty stills is the board being empty. | AX27 |
| **LP16** | Voice review with barge-in; "the value is saying no the moment you SEE the mistake" | **That is a sighted argument**, and the population that most needs voice review cannot see the mistake. LP16 also binds the referent to "the canvas selection", which presupposes a pointer. The output half — plan spoken, outcome spoken, board narrated — is the part that makes the agent a two-way channel, and it is nearly free. | AX36 |
| **CV14** | "Ghosts are the only cards on the canvas you cannot touch" | **Nor focus, name, or hear.** `GhostLayer.tsx:226-232` gives each ghost no role, no tabIndex and no label; the layer appears and vanishes with no announcement. For a non-sighted reviewer the plan has no preview at all — only the list, which is collapsed by default behind a `▴` glyph button. | AX31 |
| **DA20** | Fingerprint granularity; drop-stale-keep-rest | **Fixes the write path and not the dead end.** After `ErrStalePlan` the run returns to PROPOSED, `run.Plan.Fingerprint` is never recomputed, and the UI keeps an enabled Apply button that will fail identically forever. The computed `stale []string` goes into an event payload nothing reads. | FR11 |
| **DA21** | Idempotency; journal-then-write, diagnosed via a retried commit | **Reachable by housekeeping, not just by a retry.** Empty the trash in month two and month one's revert replays a restore of a hard-deleted element, fails partway through a 40-op inverse, leaves the earlier ops applied, writes no journal row, and never transitions the run. | JN20, FR18 |
| **PL11** | Export is structurally lossy — restore the dropped types | **The types it restores still truncate.** The renderer reads `textPreview` (`board_service.go:266-268`), so every DOCUMENT exports as its first 500 characters with no ellipsis; and images export as presigned URLs the recipient cannot fetch. | JN19 |
| **P8 / DA18** | `list_trash`, owner- vs actor-scoped, batch restore | **The human panel is worse than either says.** `Trashed` returns a flat, ungrouped, 500-capped list of every cascaded descendant, so deleting one 400-element project evicts every other recoverable item from the only restore UI in the product. | JN18 |
| **MP5** | The run lock is keyed on the root board id | **Widening the lock does not light the pill.** `BoardStateFor` is a separate read calling `ActiveByBoard(currentBoardID)`, so a collaborator working inside a nested board watches thirty elements rearrange with no pill, no name and no explanation — the exact condition `AgentObserving` was built to end, restored by W1. | JN16 |
| **DA15** | The transaction journal is never deleted | **It is also readable.** `GET /boards/:id/transactions` is registered (`router.go:46`), requires only `RequireView`, and returns `Op.UndoChanges` — the verbatim prior content of every deleted element. Empty Trash is contradicted by a live endpoint any editor can call, including one invited after the deletion. | JN21 |
| **MP2** | A public edit-link holder inherits the agent authority of an editor | **A read-only link holder can become one.** `ACL.PublicEditLink` and `ViewLink.Token` are plain `json` fields on the Element returned by `GET /boards/:id` and `?format=json`, both in the optional-auth route group. `PasswordHash` is correctly `json:"-"`, which proves the author knew how. | DL5 |
| **X1 / PL3** | Attachments: text files are rejected | **Ordinary phone photos are too.** `vision.go:29` `maxImageBytes = 4<<20` against a 3–8 MB modern camera frame; HEIC absent from `visionTypes` and from the composer's accept list; no client downscale anywhere. IN5's whiteboard-photo capability cannot receive its input, and the run proceeds describing the image by filename. | MO13 |

---

## How to read Part Two

Same law, same tiers, same discipline. **T1** = parity or a live wrong. **T2** =
collaborator. **T3** = frontier. 🔴 = a confirmed live wrong. Eight new
namespaces, one per territory: **AX** accessibility, **MO** mobile and touch,
**SC** scale and performance, **IL** instrumentation and the learning loop,
**DF** the domain, **JN** journeys, **FR** failure and recovery, **DL** data
lifecycle and privacy.

Where two territories found the same code from different directions the item is
filed once, in the corner that owns the mechanism, and marked *(two territories)*
with both consequences stated — that convergence is itself evidence, and six
items carry it. Where an item deepens or corrects a prior one it says so by id in
the heading and adds only what the original lacked.

Every claim was verified against the tree. Nineteen negative greps are
load-bearing evidence and are stated as such; the synthesis re-ran sixteen of
them plus every claim in the top ten.

---

# Corner AX — Accessibility, and the agent as an alternative channel

*Method: all three prior documents read in full, then every file in
`frontend/src/agent/`, the canvas, the shared UI primitives, all four
stylesheets, `index.html`, the manifest, `package.json`, the CI workflow, and
`digest.go`/`selfview.go`/`router.go` on the server. Contrast ratios computed
with a WCAG-2 relative-luminance script, not estimated. The load-bearing
negatives: `aria-|role=` across `*.tsx` returns **two** hits in the whole
frontend; `tabIndex` returns **zero**; `aria-live|role="status"|role="alert"`
returns **zero**; `document.title` returns **zero**; `<main>|<header>|<h1>`
returns **zero**; `:focus-visible` returns **one** rule. All re-verified.*

*The corner's own inversion, stated up front because it reorders everything in
it: for the users this territory is about, **the agent is not a feature of the
product — it is the accessible interface to it**, and the product's affordances
currently push them into the one mode that has no review (AX32).*

## T1 — parity and live wrongs

#### AX1 · `tabIndex` appears nowhere in the frontend, so no card can ever receive focus 🔴
- **Evidence** — `grep -rn "tabIndex" frontend/src --include=*.tsx` → 0 results. `ElementShell.tsx:355` renders every element as a bare `<div ref onPointerDown onContextMenu data-element-id>`; `BoardCanvas.tsx:289-301` renders the viewport as a bare `<div className="canvas-viewport">`.
- **Failure** — Selection is the precondition for the floating action bar, the resize handle, the connect anchor and the whole context menu, and selection is reachable only through `onPointerDown` or Ctrl+A. A keyboard-only or switch-access user can type into an already-open editor and press toolbar buttons; they cannot select, move, resize, connect, delete, label, lock, group or open any card. On a 2D canvas that is not degradation, it is exclusion — and it is why AX34 is the highest-leverage item in this corner rather than a nice-to-have.
- **Mechanism** — Two layers, and the second is the interesting one. (1) A roving-tabindex spatial grid on `.canvas-layer`: one element carries `tabIndex=0`, arrows move to the nearest element in that compass direction using the sizes the ResizeObserver already measures (`ElementShell.tsx:56-64` → `useView.sizes`), Home/End for reading order, Enter to open, Space to toggle selection. `role="application"` + `aria-roledescription="board canvas"` on the viewport; each `.el` a labelled `group` with `aria-describedby` carrying its cell name (the server already computes one — AX35). (2) Because arrows cannot express drag, the keyboard verbs must be **move-by-command, not move-by-pixel**: `Ctrl+arrow` nudges by the existing snap step and everything else (into a column, onto a board, connect to that card) routes to the agent's typed verbs. **The keyboard path and the agent path should be the same path.**
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (client-side). The focus model must survive the store's re-render storm (SC5) or the roving index thrashes.

#### AX2 · Nothing in the product opens or edits on a single activation — the core verbs are `onDoubleClick` 🔴 *(two territories: keyboard and touch)*
- **Evidence** — `NoteCard.tsx:129` `onDoubleClick={… startEditing()}` is the only entry to editing for an existing card; the other path is `autoEdit` at creation. Same for `DocumentCard`; board rename is context-menu only. Navigation: `cards.tsx:114` board tile, `:100` board row, `:158` LINK `window.open`, `:260` FILE, `:303` swatch — all `onDoubleClick`, each carrying `title="Double-click to open"`.
- **Failure** — The asymmetry is exact: a note created this session auto-enters editing and is writable; the same note tomorrow is not. On touch the synthesized `dblclick` races the drag that arms at 3px of finger drift (`ElementShell.tsx:89-100`), so the commonest mobile interaction is "try to open Pre-Production, move it 40px instead". The interaction grammar has no touch-expressible and no keyboard-expressible verb for OPEN. M3 is the agent's mirror of this; nobody noticed the human half.
- **Mechanism** — `Enter` on a focused shell calls the same `startEditing()`; `Escape` commits and restores focus to the shell rather than to `<body>`. `F2` renames BOARD/COLUMN. On coarse pointers a tap ending under threshold dispatches an activate intent through an optional `onActivate` prop that `BoardCard`/`LinkCard`/`FileCard` bind to the handler `onDoubleClick` already uses: first tap selects, second tap on an already-selected element activates. Mouse semantics unchanged.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** Pointer-type branching, not a global mode switch, or desktop double-click regresses.

#### AX3 · The to-do list is a keyboard trap — WCAG 2.1.2, Level A, reproducible in five seconds 🔴
- **Evidence** — `TaskListView.tsx:125-133`, verbatim: `if (e.key === 'Tab') { e.preventDefault(); const next = Math.max(0, Math.min(4, indent + (e.shiftKey ? -1 : 1))); if (next !== indent) …}` — `preventDefault()` runs **before** the clamp, so at indent 0 Shift+Tab is swallowed and at indent 4 Tab is swallowed. No Escape handler on the field.
- **Failure** — Once focus enters a task's text input, neither Tab nor Shift+Tab can leave; the exits are a mouse click elsewhere or closing the tab. Shot lists, call sheets and crew checklists are what a production board is made of, so the trap sits in the most-used element type. DA4 establishes TASKs never enter the agent's scope, so the trapped user cannot ask the agent to get them out either — the two-blind-hands pattern that recurs across this sweep (AX24).
- **Mechanism** — Only `preventDefault()` when the indent actually changes, so Tab at the boundary falls through to native focus movement. Better: move indent/outdent to `Alt+ArrowRight`/`Alt+ArrowLeft` and leave Tab alone. Expose depth as `aria-level` — today it is conveyed only by `style={{paddingLeft: indent*22}}` (`:104`), invisible to AT and hard-coded physical-left, so it also inverts under CV9.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** One condition; T1 because it is an unambiguous Level A failure.

#### AX4 · Every task checkbox is an unnamed `<button>` with no checked state — a to-do list reads as "button, edit, button, button, button"
- **Evidence** — `TaskListView.tsx:105-111` `<button className={"task-check"+(done?' done':'')} …><CheckIcon size={11}/></button>` — no `role="checkbox"`, no `aria-checked`, no label, no title. Done lives entirely in CSS (`global.css:444`). The sibling text field (`:112-134`) has no label, no placeholder, no `aria-label`. The three trailing buttons carry only English `title`s.
- **Failure** — The densest information structure in the product degrades worst: no way to hear whether a task is done, no way to hear what it says without entering its edit field, no way to distinguish the trailing icons. DA4 and P5/DA9 both touch this element type; neither touches its rendering.
- **Mechanism** — `<button role="checkbox" aria-checked={done} aria-labelledby={textId}>` reusing the text input's id, so the checkbox announces the task it belongs to. The field takes `aria-label` and `aria-level={indent+1}`; the row becomes `treeitem` inside a `tree` when any indent > 0; trailing buttons take real i18n labels. Under twenty lines, and it converts the least accessible element type into the most.
- **Hard clause** — none of the five; a rendering fix. T1 on consequence.

#### AX5 · Zero `aria-live` anywhere — the agent run is silent from ask to apply 🔴
- **Evidence** — `grep -rn "aria-live|role=\"status\"|role=\"alert\"" frontend/src` → 0. Silently-mutating surfaces: `AgentWorking.tsx:44-53` (the staged-action ticker), `:54-57` (the status phrase), `AgentDecide.tsx:114-122` ("N changes · M new" on plan arrival), `AgentDone.tsx:60-63`, `AgentObserving.tsx:29-42`, `AgentPill.tsx:16-32`. `agentStore.ts:194-201` is where every event lands; `:450` handles `action.staged`. The stream is complete, typed, and never announced.
- **Failure** — Every trust property the three prior documents exist to establish — preview before commit, honest outcomes (F3), unmet requests, per-action revert (U3), cost visibility, drift honesty — is delivered purely as a visual mutation. A blind user is told nothing when the plan arrives, nothing while forty actions stage, nothing when the transaction commits, nothing when a revert lands. The agent's entire safety story is invisible to the users for whom the agent matters most.
- **Mechanism** — Two regions with different urgency. A `role="status"` polite region carries the step ticker, throttled to ~one utterance per 1.5s so a forty-action run is not forty interruptions. A `role="alert"` assertive region carries exactly four transitions: plan ready ("Proposed: 12 changes, 4 new, 2 to trash, in Pre-Production"), applied, reverted, failed. **The announcement text must not be authored in the frontend** — see AX38.
- **Hard clause** — **digest.** The announcement string belongs to the plan contract, not to React.

#### AX6 · The AgentBar unmounts the focused element on every state transition and never places focus anywhere 🔴
- **Evidence** — `AgentBar.tsx:48-53` swaps `Observing/Pill/Ask/Working/Decide/Done` on `run.state`. `Ask` focuses its textarea on mount (`AgentAsk.tsx:70`) and nothing restores focus on unmount; `Decide` and `Done` focus nothing. `Ask` unmounts the moment `start()` flips state, so focus falls to `<body>`.
- **Failure** — Compose an intent, press Enter, focus is gone; gone again when the plan arrives; gone again on apply. For a screen-reader user, focus on `<body>` means the virtual cursor sits at the top of a document with no landmarks and no headings (AX18) — there is literally nothing to navigate back with. The accidental mitigation is instructive: the **only** reason a keyboard user can act on a proposal at all is that Apply and Discard are bound to `window` in `useAgentShell.ts:36-37`, not to any focusable control. That accident is load-bearing, and AX7 shows it also carries a live bug.
- **Mechanism** — Each state component takes a mount-focus target; the shell keeps a `returnFocus` ref. On PROPOSED focus lands on Apply — the decision the screen exists for — while the assertive region speaks the summary; on DONE it lands on the outcome row, one Tab from Undo; on discard it returns to whatever opened the composer.
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (client-side).

#### AX7 · Escape at PROPOSED discards the plan unconfirmed, and it double-fires with the panel-close handler 🔴
- **Evidence** — `useAgentShell.ts:36` `if (e.key === 'Escape' && s.run?.state === 'PROPOSED') void s.discard();` on a `window` listener. `App.tsx:151` `} else if (e.key === 'Escape') { setPanel('none'); … }` on a **separate** `window` listener. Neither calls `stopPropagation`, and App's branch has no editor guard. `ContextMenu.tsx:46`, `LabelPopover.tsx:49` and `BoardStylePopover.tsx:59` each add their own window-level Escape listener.
- **Failure** — A live defect, not only an accessibility one, and in none of the three prior documents. With a proposal pending, opening Settings or Search and pressing Escape to close it also calls `discard()` — the plan, its cost and the model turns that produced it are gone, terminally (`agentStore.ts:273` clears run and events), with no confirmation and no undo. Accessibility makes it near-certain rather than occasional: Escape is the one key a screen-reader user presses to get out of anything, they have no focus to tell them where they are, and nothing announces that the plan just died.
- **Mechanism** — A small modal/overlay stack in the view store; each listener checks it owns the top. Independently, Escape at PROPOSED should collapse or blur, not discard: discarding a plan that cost money is a decision, and decisions get the Discard button, not a reflex key.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.** The stack, not a guard — a guard on one listener will be re-broken by the next overlay.

#### AX8 · The per-action review controls are hover-exclusive, so plan adjustment does not exist on touch and is invisible to focus 🔴 *(two territories)*
- **Evidence** — `agent-bar.css:364-372` `.ap-row-x { opacity: 0 }` / `.ap-row:hover .ap-row-x, .ap-row.dropped .ap-row-x { opacity: 1 }`; duplicated at `agent.css:279-288`; same for `.ghost-chip-x` (`agent.css:112-120`) and `.task-del` (`global.css:450-451`). No `:focus-within`, no `:focus-visible` for any of them. That class is the **drop-this-action** button in review (`AgentDecide.tsx:314-317`) and the **undo-just-this-action** button after apply (`AgentDone.tsx:164-173`).
- **Failure** — The whole U-wave was built around these two affordances — "a run that got four things right and one wrong had to be thrown away entirely". On a phone, which is what `display:standalone` ships, hover never fires, so a mobile reviewer's options collapse to Apply-all or Discard-all: exactly the coarse trust bargain per-action revert existed to remove. For keyboard users the button is focusable and renders at opacity 0 — WCAG 2.4.11 and 2.4.7 failing together. And the asymmetry is perfect: `.ap-row.dropped .ap-row-x { opacity: 1 }` means *put back* is visible while *leave out* is not, so the control appears only after you have used the control you could not see.
- **Mechanism** — Add `:focus-within` to the reveal selector and drop the opacity trick entirely under `@media (hover: none), (pointer: coarse)`. Better: hover-reveal stops being the pattern for destructive-adjacent controls — persistently visible at reduced contrast in both modes. Then give the row a keyboard verb set that does not depend on reaching the button: Delete drops, `u` restores, Enter retitles, `Alt+↑/↓` reorders once CV13 lands. Pair with AX26 — a visible 18px target is still not tappable.
- **Hard clause** — none of the five; CSS and keymap. T1 because it is the gate on LP3 and on preview mode being real (AX32).

#### AX9 · The row↔ghost binding — the mechanism that makes the spatial preview legible — is mouse-only 🔴 *(two territories)*
- **Evidence** — `AgentDecide.tsx:266-267` `onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave → setHover(seq)`, no `onFocus`/`onBlur`; mirrored at `GhostLayer.tsx:230-231`, `:252-253`, `:198-206`. `hoverSeq` has no other producer. The comments at both sites state the intent: "a line of text and a rectangle in space should read as the same object."
- **Failure** — The product's own stated design principle is implemented exclusively for the mouse. Tab to a plan row and nothing lights on the canvas; on touch `hoverSeq` is permanently null, so a reviewer facing 30 rows and 12 dashed rectangles cannot ask "which rectangle is this row?" — the question review exists to answer. It matters beyond blindness: at 400% zoom the ghost and its row are never on screen together.
- **Mechanism** — `onFocus`/`onBlur` alongside the mouse handlers — four lines. Then the half worth designing: focusing a row scrolls its ghost into view (the tween machinery exists at `useAgentShell.ts:60-99`, gated on reduced motion per AX19) and announces its spatial cell; on coarse pointers the binding becomes sticky tap-to-focus in both directions, scrolling the row into `.ac-plan` and panning to the ghost. `hoverSeq` gains no new semantics — one extra producer for state the review surface already reads.
- **Hard clause** — none; composition.

#### AX10 · The nested-board tile badge is a `<span onClick>` — the depth-preview pin, the answer to M1/CV4, is not a control
- **Evidence** — `GhostLayer.tsx:193-209` `<span className="ghost-count" title onPointerDown onClick={pin} onMouseEnter onMouseLeave>` — no `<button>`, no tabIndex, no role, no key handler. The pin it sets is what opens the review list narrowed to that board (`AgentDecide.tsx:29`). `:245-261` renders each ghost's named contents as hover-only `<div>`s.
- **Failure** — M1 and CV4 identify that review is blind exactly where the agent does most of its work; the badge is the fix that shipped, and it is reachable only by mouse. The single control that makes a thirty-card, three-sub-board plan reviewable excluded the users who most need a linear review surface.
- **Mechanism** — `<button>` instead of `<span>` (it already has the click handler and the title, so this is a tag change plus `aria-pressed` and a label naming destination and count). Ghosts become `role="group"` with labels and a roving tabindex in plan order, so Tab walks the proposal.
- **Hard clause** — none; a tag change.

#### AX11 · No dialog is a dialog — and the agent's board-rules editor cannot be dismissed by keyboard at all 🔴
- **Evidence** — `Prompt.tsx:60-95` renders `.modal-backdrop`/`.modal` with no `role="dialog"`, no `aria-modal`, no `aria-labelledby` on the existing `<h3>`, no focus trap, no focus restore. Escape and Enter are handled **only** on the single-line branch (`:84`); the `multiline` branch (`:70-79`) has no `onKeyDown` at all — and `AgentAsk.tsx:123-130` opens exactly that branch for the board's `agentInstructions`. Same shape in `SearchOverlay`, `ShareDialog`, `SettingsDialog`, `TemplatePicker`.
- **Failure** — The file's own header says it replaces native dialogs because they are "unstyled, focus-stealing, unthemeable", and in doing so it dropped the three things they did correctly. Tab from inside any modal walks into the canvas behind it, which the screen reader still reads because nothing is inert. The specific casualty is agent-shaped: MP12 and CG1 both treat board `agentInstructions` as a governance surface, and its editor is the one dialog a keyboard user cannot close.
- **Mechanism** — One `<Modal>` primitive: `role="dialog" aria-modal aria-labelledby`, `inert` on the siblings of `#root`, a focus trap, Escape **on the container** (which is why the multiline branch was missed), focus restore to the invoker. Six one-line adoptions.
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (client-side); composes with AX7's overlay stack.

#### AX12 · The context menu has no menu semantics and its submenu opens on hover only — so text direction is unreachable by keyboard, by touch, AND by the agent 🔴 *(two territories; completes CV6)*
- **Evidence** — `ContextMenu.tsx:65-101` renders `<div>` + `<button>` with no `role="menu"`/`menuitem`, no focus on open, no arrow handling, no focus restore. `:70` opens the submenu on `onMouseEnter` only and `:77` `if (item.sub) return;` makes the parent's click deliberately inert. Menus open only from `onContextMenu` on non-focusable divs. The single nested item in the entire product is `t('dir.label')` → Auto/RTL/LTR (`ElementShell.tsx:254-262`).
- **Failure** — Right-click is the only home for Lock, Add label, Text direction, Group into column, Colour & icon, Rename, Create shortcut, Convert to template and Delete — plus both "Ask Qomra about this" entry points. All pointer-exclusive; on touch the long-press must additionally survive the 3px drag arm. Text direction is the sharpest case and it completes CV6 into a fact worth stating plainly: **CV6 proves the agent cannot set or see `content.textDirection`; this proves no human input modality except a hovering mouse can either.** On a bilingual product where that field also decides Arabic-Indic numeral rendering, the control that makes Arabic correct has exactly one reachable modality.
- **Mechanism** — `role="menu"`/`menuitem`/`menuitemcheckbox`, focus the first item on open, Up/Down/Home/End, Right/Enter to open a submenu, Left/Escape to close, focus restore. Parent rows with `sub` toggle on click as well as hover; on coarse pointers render `sub` items inline as an indented group. Support `Shift+F10` and the Menu key anchored to the shell's rect. And close CV6, so the capability has two hands instead of zero.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.**

#### AX13 · The shared icon set omits `aria-hidden`, so ~40 icon-only buttons have an empty accessible name
- **Evidence** — `Icons.tsx:8-19` `base()` returns width/height/viewBox/fill/stroke/… and never `aria-hidden` or `focusable="false"`. The one correct SVG in the product is a local bolt at `AgentAsk.tsx:14-21`, which **does** carry `aria-hidden="true"` — one of the two aria attributes that exist in the frontend. Unnamed: `TaskListView.tsx:105`, `Toaster.tsx:44`, `AgentDecide.tsx:314-317`, `BoardCanvas.tsx:392`, `Prompt.tsx:66`, `SearchOverlay.tsx:68`. Glyph-only: `⤢` (`AgentDecide.tsx:131`), `▾`/`▴` (`:136`), `↩` (`:312`, `AgentDone.tsx:172`), `+`/`↑` (`AgentAsk.tsx:252,263`), `☐` (`GhostLayer.tsx:264`), `✓` (`ContextMenu.tsx:96`).
- **Failure** — `title` is not a name a screen reader can be relied on to speak, and several have no title at all. `⤢` is the Show-me control that frames the proposal; `↩` is per-action revert, which CG7 calls the highest-resolution correction signal in the product. Both announce as an unpronounceable symbol or as nothing. That the author knows the pattern and applied it exactly once, to a throwaway local icon, is what makes this systemic rather than an oversight.
- **Mechanism** — Add `aria-hidden: true, focusable: false` to the `base()` spread — one line, forty sites. Then `aria-label` from i18n on every icon-only button, and a visually-hidden label beside every glyph so the glyph becomes decoration. A `.sr-only` utility does not exist in `styles/` and should.
- **Hard clause** — none; one line plus a sweep. Highest ratio of sites-fixed to lines-changed in this document.

#### AX14 · Placeholder-as-label on all three agent text fields — and the steer field's name mutates to "sent" after you use it
- **Evidence** — `AgentAsk.tsx:221-246` intent textarea: placeholder, no label, no `aria-label`. `AgentDecide.tsx:96-107` revise input: same. `AgentWorking.tsx:68-80` steer input: `placeholder={sent ? t('agent.steerSent') : t('agent.steerPlaceholder')}` — the accessible name of the control **changes as a side effect of using it**. Same pattern at `cards.tsx:215-231`, `TaskListView.tsx:46/63`, `SearchOverlay.tsx:69-75`, `Prompt.tsx:70-88`.
- **Failure** — WCAG 3.3.2 and 1.3.1: a placeholder disappears when you type, so the field's purpose is gone exactly when working memory needs it, and it is not a programmatic name at all. Compounded by AX15 — the placeholder colour is the lowest-contrast text in the product. And a control announced as a *different* control on its next focus is a genuinely novel failure.
- **Mechanism** — `aria-label` on all three from i18n keys that already exist; move the "sent" acknowledgement out of the placeholder into the polite region (AX5), which is where LP7's steering-acknowledgment argument wants it anyway.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX15 · `--ink-3` fails AA at 3.62:1 and is the colour of the agent's secondary voice, 87 declarations deep 🔴
- **Evidence** — `global.css:17` `--ink-3: #86868b` → **3.62:1** on `--surface: #ffffff`, **3.33:1** on `--canvas: #f5f5f7`; dark `#8e8e93` on `#2c2c2e` → **4.27:1**. All uses are 10–13px, so the threshold is 4.5:1. 87 declarations (agent-bar 29, agent 9, global 31, settings 18). `global.css:110` makes it the placeholder colour, i.e. every field's only label. Also failing: `--danger #ff3b30` on white = **3.55:1** (used for `.ac-warn`, the destructive-count warning), `--ok #34c759` = **2.22:1**, comment card `#b3930a` on `#fffbea` = **2.85:1**, and hard-coded `#9a97a5` ≈ 3.1:1 at `SearchOverlay.tsx:82/84`.
- **Failure** — Not a token nit. `.ac-decide-text` ("12 changes · 4 new"), `.ap-kind` (every attribute-edit action's classification) and `.ghost-count` (the count going into each nested board) are all `--ink-3`. The summary of what you are approving, the classification of each action, and the depth roll-up are rendered in the one colour that fails — in Oman's daylight, on a laptop on location.
- **Mechanism** — Retune `--ink-3` to ≥4.5:1 in both themes (≈`#6e6e73` / `#a1a1a6`) and demote today's values to `--ink-4` for genuinely decorative text. `--danger` needs a text token separate from its fill token (`#d70015` is 5.3:1 and reads identically as a red). Delete the two hard-coded literals. Add a build-time contrast assertion over the token pairs — fifteen lines, and the pairs are enumerable.
- **Hard clause** — **probe.** Without the assertion this regresses on the next palette change.

#### AX16 · The focus indicator IS the accent, the accent fails 1.4.11 in dark mode, the accent is user-selectable with no floor, and three inputs suppress the outline entirely 🔴
- **Evidence** — Exactly one `:focus-visible` rule exists in all four stylesheets (`agent-bar.css:584`). `--accent: #5e5ce6` on dark `--surface` → **2.75:1**, on dark `--canvas` → **3.36:1**; it is the ring at `agent.css:190` and the field border at `agent-bar.css:598`. Suppressed with no replacement: `agent.css:277` `.ap-row-input:focus { outline: none }` — the plan-row retitle field — and `agent.css:56` `.ghost-title-input:focus`. The accent is user-set from nine swatches (`settingsStore.ts:69-72`, `SettingsDialog.tsx:308`); `#f2a20d` on dark surface is under 2:1 as a ring.
- **Failure** — WCAG 1.4.11 requires 3:1. In dark mode the product's only focus indicator sits below it, and on two agent-editable fields there is none at all — a keyboard user retitling a plan row cannot see that they are in the field. Compounds AX6 into: focus is neither placed nor visible.
- **Mechanism** — A global `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--focus-ring); outline-offset: 2px }` with `--focus-ring` a **dedicated** token verified ≥3:1 against `--surface`, `--surface-2` and `--canvas` in both themes — keeping the accent for identity and decoupling the user's colour choice from a WCAG floor. Remove the two bare `outline: none`. Optionally clamp `accentColor` at set time; `applySideEffects` already computes `shade()` and `hexToRgb`.
- **Hard clause** — **probe**, shared with AX15.

#### AX17 · "This card will be moved or deleted" is 38% opacity and nothing else — and the dimming destroys the contrast of the text being reviewed 🔴
- **Evidence** — `agent.css:125` `.el.agent-proposed { opacity: 0.38 }`, set from `GhostLayer.tsx:274-288` for `move_element`, `place` and every destructive kind. No class-independent signal: no `aria-*`, no icon, no border, no text.
- **Failure** — WCAG 1.4.1 on the highest-stakes review in the product — the cards about to go to trash. The second-order effect is worse: 38% multiplies through to the card's own text, so `--ink` at 16.8:1 becomes roughly 3.3:1. The user is asked to read and approve content the preview has made hard to read, and for a low-vision user the dimmed cards are simply gone. The visual mirror of CV4, in none of the three prior documents.
- **Mechanism** — Keep the dim as ambient signal and stop relying on it: a persistent non-colour badge reading the action kind ("→ moving", "trash") — the same string `kindLabel()` already produces for the ghost head — plus `aria-describedby` on the shell. Reduce the dim to ~0.62 and carry the rest in a dashed border matching the ghost treatment, so "proposal" reads as one visual language across the ghost layer and the live layer.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** The badge string must come from the same kind map the ghost head uses, or the two drift.

#### AX18 · No landmarks, no headings, and `document.title` never changes — navigating to another board is completely silent
- **Evidence** — `grep -rn "document.title" frontend/src` → 0 while `boardStore.ts:113/154` maintains `boardTitle` for the chrome. `<main>|<header>|<h1>` → 0; one `<nav>` (SettingsDialog's tab list). The breadcrumb is `<div className="breadcrumbs">` with `<button>` children and the current board as a bare `<span>`. `App.tsx:107` `navigate()` swaps the entire store and the realtime room with no announcement and no focus move.
- **Failure** — Board navigation in this product *is* page navigation — the whole canvas is replaced — and it produces no title change, no focus change, no announcement, and lands in a document with no headings to orient by. Combined with AX6, "go to the top and start over" is not a usable recovery because the top of the document has nothing in it.
- **Mechanism** — `document.title` on `openBoard`; `<h1 class="sr-only">{boardTitle}</h1>`; `<nav aria-label>` + `<ol>` for the breadcrumb with `aria-current="page"`; `<main>` around the viewport, `<nav>` on the rail, `<header>` on the topbar, `<aside>` on the panels; skip links to canvas and agent bar; focus moves to the `<h1>` after `navigate()` resolves. The cheapest item in this corner, and it unblocks orientation for everything else.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX19 · Not one of nine GSAP animations respects `prefers-reduced-motion` — including the pan-and-zoom the agent fires on every plan arrival 🔴
- **Evidence** — Nine JS-driven animations: `useAgentShell.ts:95-98` (0.55s canvas pan+zoom on plan arrival), `BoardCanvas.tsx:91-94` (fit-all), `GhostLayer.tsx:128-132`, `AgentBar.tsx:39`, `ElementShell.tsx:69`, `BoardsDrawer.tsx:29`, `TrashPanel.tsx:23`, `UnsortedTray.tsx:19`, `Toolbar.tsx:38`. None reads a media query. The only reduced-motion handling is two CSS blocks (`agent-bar.css:392-396`, `agent.css:150-152`); `global.css` has none, leaving `fadeIn`, `popIn`, `toastIn` and `spin` unguarded. The only `matchMedia` in the codebase is for `prefers-color-scheme`.
- **Failure** — The two worst offenders are the ones CSS can never reach, because they are viewport transforms driven from JS. `frameProposal()` pans **and** zooms the whole canvas whenever a plan arrives — an unrequested, full-viewport, 0.55-second motion event, precisely the WCAG 2.3.3 vestibular trigger. The team clearly knows the query (it appears twice), which makes this a scoping error rather than an unknown.
- **Mechanism** — `settingsStore.applySideEffects` already stamps five root attributes and already subscribes to one OS media query; add `data-motion="reduced|full"` plus an explicit override (AX30). Export one `tween()` wrapper all nine sites use: under reduced motion it `gsap.set`s the end state. **Crucially `frameProposal` and `fitAll` must still JUMP to the target** — the framing is information (the plan is off-screen); only the traversal is decoration. Add the missing `global.css` block.
- **Hard clause** — **handler** (client-side). The wrapper must be the only tween entry point or the tenth animation reintroduces it.

#### AX20 · `z` is a single-character global shortcut that retargets the entire canvas, unremappable and undisableable
- **Evidence** — `BoardCanvas.tsx:97-108` `if (e.key.toLowerCase() === 'z' && !e.ctrlKey && !e.metaKey) fitAll();` on a `window` listener guarded only by a text-field check. Space is captured as a global pan modifier at `:101`/`:104` with the same single guard.
- **Failure** — WCAG 2.1.4 requires a single-character shortcut to be remappable, disableable, or focus-scoped; this is none of the three. Speech-input users emit stray characters constantly, and every stray `z` re-frames and re-zooms the board — which under AX19 is also an unguarded motion event. The Space capture is a second instance: Space is the standard activation key for buttons, so `spaceDown.current` flips true whenever Space activates any focused button on the page.
- **Mechanism** — Scope both to the canvas by requiring `document.activeElement` within `.canvas-viewport` (which AX1 makes meaningful), or move fit-all to `Shift+1`. Register all global shortcuts in one table so they can be listed in Settings and later remapped — the keymap is spread across `App.tsx:117-153`, `BoardCanvas.tsx:97-108` and `useAgentShell.ts:25-38`, and consolidating it is also what AX7 needs.
- **Hard clause** — **handler.**

#### AX21 · The one correct ARIA in the product has no accessible name — every Settings switch announces as "switch, off"
- **Evidence** — `SettingsDialog.tsx:97` `<button role="switch" aria-checked={on} onClick=… />` with no label; its text lives in a sibling `<div className="sr-label">` (`:84-93`) with no `id` and no `aria-labelledby`. Sixteen switches use it, including `showEmailToOthers` (`:570`) and `showPresence` (`:567`) — privacy settings. `Segmented` (`:100-110`) conveys selection only via a class; accent swatches (`:330-339`) are named by a hex code.
- **Failure** — ARIA reached for exactly once, and reached halfway: `role="switch"` without a name is worse than a plain checkbox in a label, which would have been correct with no ARIA at all. A blind user can hear that sixteen switches exist and the state of each, and cannot learn what any controls. The same state-blindness runs through every toggle group — label-filter chips, search scope buttons, and the agent's own scope and mode chips (`AgentAsk.tsx:271-286`), where the chip that says whether this run will PREVIEW or AUTO-APPLY carries its state in a CSS class.
- **Mechanism** — `Row` generates an id and passes it down; `Switch` takes `aria-labelledby` (+ `aria-describedby` for the sub line). `Segmented` becomes a radiogroup or keeps buttons with `aria-pressed`. Swatches take colour names, not hex. The autonomy chip additionally needs a spoken consequence — "applies without asking" is a safety statement (MP1/MP2) that currently exists only as a `title` and a colour.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX22 · The toast stack is not a live region — every async failure is announced to nobody and disappears in four seconds
- **Evidence** — `Toaster.tsx:34-47` renders `<div className="toaster">` with no role and no `aria-live`; auto-dismiss at 4000ms (`:23`). It is the universal error channel: `ElementShell.tsx:141-142` ("Moved N items to Unsorted" / "Move failed"), `AgentAsk.tsx:102`, and the API layer generally. `SearchOverlay.tsx:82-85` renders "Searching…"/"No matches." as plain divs; `NotificationsBell.tsx:57-59` renders the unread count in a `<span>` on a button named by a static English `title`.
- **Failure** — A blind user meets the toast mostly as the report on an action they *did* take — and hears nothing. Four seconds is below the WCAG 2.2.1 threshold regardless of AT. The notification badge is the collaboration channel MP8/MP9/MP10 spend three findings on; its delivery surface never speaks.
- **Mechanism** — Two containers, because one cannot vary politeness: `role="status"` for info/success, `role="alert"` for errors, with errors persisting until dismissed. `aria-label` on the bell carrying the count, and announcement of new arrivals through the polite region on the `qomra:notification` event already listened for.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX23 · Global search results are `<div onClick>` — the one cross-board discovery surface is mouse-only except "Enter opens the first hit"
- **Evidence** — `SearchOverlay.tsx:86-94` `<div className="search-result" onClick={() => void open(el)}>` — not a button, no tabIndex, no key handler. The only keyboard path is `:74`, which opens `visible[0]`. Count and searching state are never announced.
- **Failure** — Search is the one navigation surface that does not require a pointer on the canvas, which makes it the natural primary interface for a keyboard user — and it terminates in a list they cannot walk. It also means the fallback for AX1 ("if you cannot navigate the canvas, find things by name") does not exist. DA8 argues the index is under-used by the agent; this is the human half of the same surface being under-built.
- **Mechanism** — `<button>` per result, or `role="listbox"`/`option` with `aria-activedescendant` from the input — the combobox pattern the UI already looks like. Announce the result count on debounce settle and the destination board on activation (pairs with AX18).
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX24 · A capability set reachable by NEITHER pointer-free input NOR the agent — and it contains the protection primitive 🔴
- **Evidence** — Pointer-only via AX12: Lock (`ElementShell.tsx:252`), Text direction (`:254-262`), Group into column (`:263`), Convert to template (`:289-293`), Create shortcut (`:288`). Agent-blind, verified by grep against the registry: `locked|isTemplate|textDirection|strokes` in `tools.go`/`toolhandlers.go`/`digest.go` → **zero hits**, and the 50-name tool table contains no lock, direction, template or sketch verb. Freehand drawing is pointer-only by construction; CV23 establishes the agent can neither make nor read a sketch; P1 establishes the agent ignores locks; DA5 establishes it cannot read lines.
- **Failure** — The three prior documents treat "the agent cannot do X" as a capability gap. The new fact is that for a specific set of X, **the agent is the only remaining hand and it does not have it either.** Lock is the load-bearing case: it is the product's protection primitive, it is right-click-only, and P1 shows the agent overrides it — so a user who cannot right-click cannot protect anything from an agent that does not respect the protection anyway. Text direction on a bilingual product is the second. This set is the acceptance criterion for AX34.
- **Mechanism** — Two moves that must land together. (1) Every context-menu action gets a keyboard route (AX12) — necessary but not sufficient, because switch-access and voice users may still not reach it. (2) Every context-menu action gets a typed agent verb: `set_lock`, `set_direction` (closes CV6), `set_template`, `group_into_column` (closes CV12 at one row instead of N+1), and a declarative `create_sketch` taking a stroke spec. Then the standing rule that makes it durable and is the real deliverable: **no gesture ships without a typed verb behind it** — the five-clause discipline the agent already lives under, extended to the pointer.
- **Hard clause** — **schema.** The verb set is the deliverable; the discipline is what keeps the set closed.

## T2 — collaborator

#### AX25 · Four media queries exist in 2,405 lines of CSS and three belong to the agent bar *(two territories)*
- **Evidence** — `grep -n "@media" frontend/src/styles/*.css` → agent-bar 3 (two width, one reduced-motion), agent 1 (reduced-motion), **global.css 0, settings.css 0**. `body { overflow: hidden }` (`global.css:96`); `--rail-w: 76px`, `--topbar-h: 52px` fixed; `.side-panel` 336px; `.modal` 540px; `body { font-size: 14px }` with 8 `rem` uses against several hundred `px`. `manifest.webmanifest` declares `"display": "standalone"`.
- **Failure** — WCAG 1.4.10 Reflow and 1.4.4 Resize Text: `overflow:hidden` on body means content outside the viewport is unreachable rather than scrollable, and the root font size ignores the browser's. On a 375px phone the permanent rail takes 20% of the width and opening the Unsorted tray — the capture inbox, the thing a phone is *for* — leaves 39px of canvas. The part that belongs in this document rather than a CSS one: **the agent bar is the only surface in the product ever laid out for a small screen**, which makes the agent-first mobile argument (MO17) empirical rather than rhetorical.
- **Mechanism** — Three structural rules, not a redesign: below ~640px the rail becomes a bottom bar (which also puts the trash drop-target in thumb reach), `.side-panel` becomes `min(336px, 100%)`, modals become full-bleed sheets. Type moves to `rem` off a root that honours the browser, keeping `[data-density]` as a multiplier. Reflow test: 320×256 CSS px, no horizontal scroll, every control reachable.
- **Hard clause** — none; a layout project with one exclusion (the canvas layer keeps physical coordinates, exactly as CV9 requires).

#### AX26 · Targets from 14px to 22px across the primary review and editing controls, on a product that installs as a phone app *(two territories)*
- **Evidence** — `.ap-row-x` 18×18 (drop / put-back), `.ghost-chip-x` 18×18, `.task-check` 18×18, `.resize-handle` **14×14**, `.connect-anchor` **15×15**, `.ac-file button` 16×16, `.drift-no` 22×22, `.ghost-dissolve` 20×20, `.avatar` 27×27, `.el-actions button` 30×30, `.topbar-btn.icon-only` 32px, `.zoom-cluster button` 34×34. Largest is 34; WCAG 2.5.8 AA floor is 24 and both platform HIGs say 44.
- **Failure** — The distribution is the tell: the **smallest** targets are the highest-stakes — 18px to drop an agent action or undo one, 14px to resize, 15px to draw a connector — because they were sized for a cursor's single-pixel hotspot. `.el-actions` sits at `top:-42px`, so a card near the top of a phone viewport has its Duplicate/Delete bar off-screen. Compounded by AX8: the 18px drop button is also invisible until hover.
- **Mechanism** — Expand hit area without changing visual size: `@media (pointer: coarse)` adding `::before { inset: -13px }` to the small circular controls and `min-width/min-height: 44px` to the button classes. `.el-actions` flips below the card when the card's top is within 50px of the viewport top. The resize handle and connect anchor additionally need keyboard equivalents (AX1's `Ctrl+arrow`, a `connect` verb), because no target size makes a pointer-only gesture reachable without a pointer.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX27 · Uncaptioned images are `alt=""` — a 40-still mood board is 40 decorative images — and a caption is announced twice *(corrects IN15)*
- **Evidence** — `cards.tsx:214` `<img src alt={c.caption ?? ''} …>` immediately followed by `:215-231`, the visible caption `<input>` — so with a caption the same text is the alt **and** the field's value; without one the image asserts to AT that it carries no information. `cards.tsx:62` (board tile) and `:159` (link thumbnail) are `alt=""` unconditionally. `SketchCard` renders a bare `<svg>` with no `role="img"` and no `<title>`; `LineLayer.tsx:314/339` renders the connector graph with no accessible representation, though lines carry a `label` and a `relation`.
- **Failure** — IN15 undersells its own case twice. First: this codebase **already** wires caption→alt, so `caption_images(ids[])` is not an accessibility lens, it is the alt-text pipeline, complete except for the batch call. Second: on a filmmaker's reference board the images ARE the content, so `alt=""` on forty stills is the board being empty. The double-announcement and the unlabelled caption field are new; and SketchCard/LineLayer compound with CV23/DA5 into AX24's pattern — the diagram semantics are invisible to AT and to the agent simultaneously.
- **Mechanism** — The image and the caption field must not both speak: label the input properly and give the `<img>` an accessible name only when the caption is absent. Ship IN15 and say what it is for: the digest flag that notices thirty uncaptioned images is also an accessibility audit. `SketchCard` gets `role="img"` + `<title>` (the same string CV23's read capability would produce). The connector graph gets a linear text rendering — "Casting → Budget, blocks" — exactly the shape DA5's `edgesAmong` was written to produce and which is currently dead code.
- **Hard clause** — **digest**, shared with IN15.

#### AX28 · The RTL gap is not only CSS — nine physical-pixel JS transforms and five directional glyphs survive CV9's fix *(corrects CV9)*
- **Evidence** — Not covered by CV9: `BoardsDrawer.tsx:29` `gsap.fromTo(panel, {x:-340})`, `TrashPanel.tsx:23` / `UnsortedTray.tsx:19` `{x:340}`, `Toolbar.tsx:38` `{x:-8}`; `TaskListView.tsx:104` `paddingLeft: indent*22` and `:167` `marginLeft: 27 + indent*22`; `ElementShell.tsx:317-323` and `global.css:305/313` `right:-5px`/`right:-8px`. Glyphs as sole content: `→` (`AgentDecide.tsx:289`, `AgentDone.tsx:159`), `▾`/`▴`, `⤢`, `↩`. `manifest.webmanifest` declares neither `lang` nor `dir`.
- **Failure** — CV9's mechanism is necessary and insufficient: shipping it alone produces a mirrored app with three panels sliding in from the wrong side and task nesting indenting the wrong way. The screen-reader half is new territory entirely: `→` before a destination is the **only** thing that says where a plan row lands, and it is announced as "right arrow", "rightwards arrow", or nothing depending on punctuation verbosity — so in both languages, in both directions, the destination relationship is lost in speech. That is CV8/CV15's review-legibility argument failing on the audio channel.
- **Mechanism** — Extend CV9's scope: AX19's tween wrapper takes a logical sign (`dirSign() * 340`); the task indent moves to `paddingInlineStart`; anchor offsets move to `inset-inline-end`. `→` becomes a visually-hidden "into" plus an `aria-hidden` glyph — which fixes the LTR-arrow-in-RTL problem for free, since a hidden glyph cannot point the wrong way. `lang` and `dir` in the manifest. One probe worth writing: with `dir=rtl` on `documentElement`, the ~18 correct `dir="auto"` content sites must still resolve per element — the canvas exclusion boundary CV9 names is exactly where a bidi bug would hide.
- **Hard clause** — none; the risk is the exclusion boundary, as in CV9.

#### AX29 · Silent DOM mutations from other people: a colleague's agent run, remote cursors, and remote-edit badges are all unannounced or over-announced
- **Evidence** — `AgentObserving.tsx:29-42` renders a `title` on a non-interactive div — which AT does not reliably expose — to announce that another user's agent is rewriting the board you are on; no live region. `BoardCanvas.tsx:323-328` renders every remote cursor as a positioned `<div>` containing a name, mutating on every peer pointer move, inside the canvas layer. `ElementShell.tsx:356` renders `{remoteEditor} is editing…` in hardcoded English.
- **Failure** — Presence is simultaneously unannounced where it matters (someone is changing your board) and over-present where it does not (a name following a mouse, strewn through the board content in browse mode). MP16 argues the agent has no presence and never claims what it is about to rewrite; `AgentObserving` is the answer that shipped, and it is inaudible.
- **Mechanism** — `aria-hidden="true"` on the remote-cursor layer — it is a pointer affordance with no meaning without one — and expose presence instead as a topbar summary with polite announcements on join/leave. Route `AgentObserving`'s arrival and its PROPOSED transition through the polite region, since MP15 and MP6 make "the board is in motion" a safety statement. `remote-edit-badge` needs i18n and a shell-scoped live region.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX30 · Settings has five root-attribute channels and zero accessibility options — the plumbing every fix here needs is already used for dot grids
- **Evidence** — `settingsStore.ts:63-74 applySideEffects` sets `data-theme`, `data-density`, `data-dotgrid`, `data-shadows` and four accent properties on `documentElement`, and `:39` already subscribes to `prefers-color-scheme`. Tabs: Account, Notifications, Appearance, Preferences, Toolbar, Privacy. No accessibility tab, no motion control, no text-size control, no contrast control, no announcement-verbosity control.
- **Failure** — Not capability, intent: the product will let a user turn off card shadows and cannot let them turn off a full-viewport camera tween. Stating it as a finding converts a scattered list of CSS fixes into one product surface with one owner, and gives the user a place to say "announce every staged action" or "announce only the outcome" — a preference no default can guess.
- **Mechanism** — An Accessibility tab writing `data-motion`, `data-contrast`, `data-textscale` and an `announceVerbosity` preference consumed by AX5's regions, each defaulting to the OS signal (`prefers-reduced-motion`, `prefers-contrast`, `prefers-reduced-transparency` — the glass surfaces at `global.css:11/104` with `backdrop-filter: blur(24px) saturate(180%)` are a direct hit for the last) with an explicit override, exactly as `theme` already does. These persist through the same server-merged settings object as everything else, which matters more for accessibility settings than for dot grids.
- **Hard clause** — none.

#### AX31 · Ghosts cannot be focused, named or announced — so for a non-sighted reviewer the plan has no preview at all *(deepens CV14)*
- **Evidence** — `GhostLayer.tsx:226-232` renders each ghost as a styled `<div>` with `onPointerDown` stopPropagation and hover handlers — no role, no tabIndex, no label; `:239-241` the body; `:246-261` named contents as hover-only divs; `:263-265` staged tasks as `☐ {t}`, a glyph carrying the checkbox semantics. `:138` the layer returns null unless PROPOSED, so it appears and vanishes with no announcement.
- **Failure** — The ghost layer is the product's central claim — "what you approve is positioned identically to what commits" — and it is a purely visual assertion. For a blind user the plan has a **list**, not a preview, and that list is a secondary surface opened by a `▴` glyph button and collapsed by default. The accessibility answer inverts the product's design: the list is the canonical review surface for AT and the canvas is the enhancement — which means the list has to carry everything the canvas carries.
- **Mechanism** — `role="group"` + label on each ghost, roving tabindex in plan order, `aria-describedby` carrying the spatial cell. On the list side: depth as `aria-level` rather than inline padding (`AgentDecide.tsx:263` already computes it), dropped rows as `aria-disabled` **plus text**, destructive rows named with a word rather than a `.danger` class, and the list always rendered — visually hidden when the canvas preview is the active surface — so the two can never diverge.
- **Hard clause** — **compiler.** The list and the ghost layer must project from one structure, or "always rendered" becomes "two renderers that disagree".

#### AX33 · No a11y lint, no axe, no assertion in CI — while a real-DOM harness for every agent surface already exists and is one line away
- **Evidence** — `frontend/package.json` devDependencies: vite, vitest, jsdom, typescript, react types. No eslint (despite surviving `eslint-disable-next-line` comments at `AgentAsk.tsx:72` and `App.tsx:105`, which are therefore dead), no `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`, no `@axe-core/react`, no testing-library. CI frontend job = `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run --passWithNoTests`, `vite build`. Meanwhile `frontend/src/agent/render.test.tsx:1-40` builds a real `createRoot` harness whose header argues precisely the right thing — "typechecking proves the shapes line up; it does not prove a component survives being asked to draw itself" — then renders Pill, Observing, Working, Decide, Done, PlanNotes and GhostLayer for real.
- **Failure** — The team already built and justified the exact harness an accessibility assertion needs, for the exact components that need it, and stopped one assertion short. Part One's amendment — a probe must assert a rendered result, not a compiled op — has an obvious next term: **a rendered result no assistive technology can perceive is the same class of lie as a write that lands on a key nothing reads.** M5 says the corpus never leaves flatland; MP11 says every probe is single-owner; this is the third axis — every probe is sighted.
- **Mechanism** — `@axe-core/react` inside the existing container, failing on serious/critical per agent state. `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` would have caught AX10's clickable span, AX23's clickable divs, AX21's unnamed switch and every unlabelled input here, statically, at zero runtime cost. Add AX15's contrast fixture. Then the clause: **a surface that renders a plan must assert its accessible name and its live-region text**, so AX5 cannot silently regress.
- **Hard clause** — **probe.** Belongs in WAVE 0 with CG4 and MP11.

## T3 — frontier

#### AX32 · Auto-apply is currently the only accessible way to use the agent — the safe path is the one that excludes
- **Evidence** — Preview mode terminates in the Decide screen, whose review depends on the hover-only drop button (AX8), the hover-only row↔ghost binding (AX9), the mouse-only tile badge (AX10), a list collapsed behind a `▴` glyph (AX13), no focus placement (AX6), no announcement (AX5) and an Escape that discards without confirmation (AX7). Auto mode (`AgentAsk.tsx:279-286`) skips all of it and lands in `Done`, which needs no review interaction.
- **Mechanism** — Treat "the review is operable without a pointer" as a shipping gate on preview mode: AX5, AX6, AX8, AX9, AX10 and AX31 **are** that gate, and together they are roughly two days. Then the product move: an **accessible review mode** that is not the canvas at all — a linear, announced, keyboard-driven walk of the plan where each step speaks "3 of 12: create card 'Sunrise scout' into Locations" and takes keep / drop / retitle / send-elsewhere as single keys. That is the same closed Adjustment vocabulary in a modality where it works; it is exactly the surface LP16's voice review must bind to; and it is MO19's sequence review with a different renderer — build it once and three land. The load-bearing irony for LP9: any autonomy-graduation work that does not fix the review surface first is graduating people into the unsafe mode **for accessibility reasons**.

#### AX34 · The agent as a full alternative input channel — and the specific gate that must close before the claim is true
- **Evidence** — The substrate exists: 50 typed verbs, read-live/write-staged, one-transaction commit, per-run and per-action revert, delegation attenuation, a scope selector including `selection`. What blocks it: AX24's pointer-only ∩ agent-blind set; TASKs invisible (DA4); LINEs invisible (DA5); comments unreadable (DA6); and the entry point's own trap — `selection` scope only appears when something is already selected, which requires a pointer (AX1).
- **Mechanism** — Three things in order. (1) Close AX24's set; the acceptance test is literally "enumerate every context-menu and toolbar item and show a verb for each". (2) Give scope a nameable form — "the three cards I just made", "everything in Locations", "what's on screen" — resolved server-side as `scope: 'described'`, replacing the pointer-selection dependency (the viewport is already in the digest; CV22 notes the label filter is not). (3) Make the agent's answers reachable: a run's prose lands in `.ac-summary` and is destroyed by the next run, so the alternative channel has no memory — **LP10's agent inbox is the accessibility prerequisite it does not know it is.**

#### AX35 · Board-as-narrative already exists server-side, is spoken only to the model, and has no human read path
- **Evidence** — `digest.go:1090-1121 regions()` produces "12 around C3, 8 around B2, 5 around A1"; `:1059-1084 cellName` builds the stable neighbourhood names; the comment at `:767-771` states the intent — "Neighbourhoods, not coordinates: people describe boards as 'the cluster on the left'". `selfview.go:44-60 RenderSelfView` runs the committed layout and "describes the outcome in the terms a person would use looking at the board". Both are consumed only by the planner. The HTTP surface exposes capabilities, runs, events, apply, audit, board-state, refine, discard, cancel, revert, retry, continue, steer — and no describe.
- **Mechanism** — `GET /agent/boards/:id/describe` returning the digest's human-facing subset — regions, counts by type, containers and their contents, the tree — with **no model call**, therefore no cost, no latency and no spend governance to negotiate. Consumed in three places: `aria-describedby` on the viewport, a spoken board tour (AX37), and the `aria-label` of each element under AX1's roving focus, which is where `cellName` earns its keep. Two clauses: the description must be generated from the **same walk** the digest uses so the two cannot drift (CV10's shared-fixture discipline), and it must respect the same ACL, since MP4 shows `attachAncestry` already reads ancestors without a check. *The hardest question in this territory — how does a blind user perceive a 2D board — was already answered for a different reader, and the expensive part is done.*

#### AX36 · Voice OUT is the missing half — X4 and LP16 both scope voice to input *(corrects LP16)*
- **Evidence** — `grep -rn "SpeechRecognition|speechSynthesis|getUserMedia|MediaRecorder" frontend/src backend/internal` → zero hits anywhere in the product. X4 is input-only. LP16 correctly observes that dictation is the less valuable half and moves to voice review with barge-in — but binds it to the Adjustment verbs and to "the canvas selection as the referent", which presupposes the sighted canvas and a pointer-made selection.
- **Mechanism** — Ship the output half first; it is nearly free and composes with everything. `speechSynthesis` reading (a) the plan summary and counts on PROPOSED, (b) the outcome and unmet list on completion, (c) on request the board narrative from AX35 — with `lang` from the i18n language, since a browser TTS voice given Arabic text under `lang="en"` produces gibberish (so U5/CV9's language work is a hard prerequisite). Then LP16's barge-in becomes coherent — interruptibility matters **more** when the spoken summary is the user's only view of the plan — and its two hard rules hold unchanged. The one addition this territory demands: the referent must also accept ordinal and name reference ("drop number three", "drop the Sunrise one"), which is AX34's `scope: 'described'` resolver again.

#### AX37 · A screen-reader board tour as a read-only agent register — the agent as the cursor, not the narrator
- **Evidence** — The pieces exist: `regions()`/`cellName` (AX35), the viewport already in the digest, `GET /agent/boards/:id/state` as a free model-less read already used for the drift nudge, and IN17's proposed foveated tiers. What does not exist is any read-only navigational register — every workload terminates in a staged plan, and `toolFinish` plus `plan.summary` is the only human-facing output.
- **Mechanism** — Nobody perceives a board all at once; sighted users foveate, pan and zoom. IN17 already models that **for the model**. The unmade observation is that IN17's three fidelity tiers ARE the structure of a screen-reader tour: focused is what you read now, blurry is what you can move to, clustered is the overview you orient by. So: a read-only register — no writes, no staging, no plan, therefore outside the delegation and spend paths entirely — driven from the canvas by keyboard: overview, enter a cluster, walk its items, ask about one ("what connects to this?" — DA5's `edgesAmong` finally having a reader), jump to a named thing. Two clauses. It must be **free at the common case** (overview and walk come from the digest/state read; only an open-ended question spends). And it must be honest about elision: if the walk was capped, the tour says so, because a blind user has no peripheral vision to notice that a description stopped short — CV21's honesty rule, where the difference is between a description and a lie.

#### AX38 · Announcement text is a plan concern being treated as a frontend one
- **Evidence** — `api/types.ts:365-373` `AgentEvent { type, message?, data? }` is server-written, and `AgentWorking.tsx:36-40` says so explicitly — "A staged action carries the server's own sentence about what it decided. That is the one string here worth more than a translated placeholder" — preferring `latest.message` over the i18n phrase. `AgentWorking.tsx:18-26 phraseKeyFor` is a **substring matcher** over tool names (`'read board'`, `'search'`, `'create'`, `'move'`) degrading to "Thinking…".
- **Mechanism** — This decides whether AX5 ages well.
